   25: # SQL comparison operators that are safe to interpolate into WHERE clauses.
   26: # Scalar-binding operators: the value is always passed as a bind param (?), so
   27: # no quoting or escaping is required on the value side.  LIKE and NOT LIKE are
   28: # safe for the same reason â€” the pattern is bound, not interpolated.
   29: Readonly::Hash my %SAFE_SQL_OPS => map { $_ => 1 } ('>', '<', '>=', '<=', '!=', '=', 'LIKE', 'NOT LIKE');
   30: 
   31: # List-membership operators whose criterion value is an arrayref.
   32: # Each element is bound as a separate ?, so injection is impossible.
   33: # IN  with an empty list â†’ WHERE 1=0 (no rows match, correct SQL semantics).
   34: # NOT IN with an empty list â†’ no WHERE term (all rows match, correct semantics).
   35: Readonly::Hash my %SAFE_LIST_OPS => map { $_ => 1 } ('IN', 'NOT IN');
   36: 
   37: # Nullability operators: no bind parameter.  The hashref value is ignored â€”
   38: # any value (undef, 1, ...) signals intent; only the key selects the operator.
   39: # A bare undef criterion value (col => undef) also generates IS NULL.
   40: Readonly::Hash my %SAFE_NOARG_OPS => map { $_ => 1 } ('IS NULL', 'IS NOT NULL');

  107: 
  108: =head1 NAME
  109: 
  110: Database::Join - Read-only combined view across two or more Database::Abstraction objects
  111: 
  112: =head1 VERSION
  113: 
  114: Version 0.011.0
  115: 
  116: =head1 SYNOPSIS
  117: 
  118: B<Basic two-database join>
  119: 
  120:     use Database::Join;
  121: 
  122:     # Step 1: create each component database the normal way
  123:     # See Database::Abstraction
  124:     my $customers = Database::Customers->new(directory => '/data');
  125:     my $loyalty   = Database::Loyalty->new(directory  => '/data');
  126: 
  127:     # Step 2: combine them on the shared key column 'entry'
  128:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  129:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  130:         join_column => 'entry',
  131:     );
  132: 
  133:     # Step 3: query exactly as you would a single Database::Abstraction object
  134:     my $all_rows  = $join->selectall_arrayref();
  135:     my $vip_rows  = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');
  136:     my $one_row   = $join->fetchrow_hashref(entry => 'C001');
  137:     my $total     = $join->count();
  138:     my $col_names = $join->columns();
  139: 
  140: B<Hiding internal columns>
  141: 
  142:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  143:         databases      => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  144:         join_column    => 'entry',
  145:         remove_columns => [ 'internal_id', 'audit_ts' ],
  146:     );
  147:     # 'internal_id' and 'audit_ts' never appear in results or columns()
  148: 
  149: B<join_map: when the key column has different names in each database>
  150: 
  151:     # $cities  (index 0) has a column called 'statecode' -- matches join_column
  152:     # $stnames (index 1) has a column called 'entry'     -- different name
  153: 
  154:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  155:         databases   => [ $cities,  $stnames ],
  156:         #                index 0   index 1
  157:         join_column => 'statecode',
  158:         join_map    => { 1 => 'entry' },  # index 1 calls its join key 'entry'
  159:     );
  160: 
  161:     # All returned rows use 'statecode'; 'entry' is never exposed
  162:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
  163: 
  164: B<filters: permanently restrict a database's visible rows>
  165: 
  166:     # Only show orders placed more than 60 days ago, without repeating
  167:     # the criterion on every query call.
  168:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  169:         databases   => [ $customers, $orders ],
  170:         join_column => 'entry',
  171:         filters     => { 1 => { age_days => { '>' => 60 } } },
  172:     );
  173: 
  174:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();                 # all old orders
  175:     my $vip  = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');   # old + gold tier
  176: 
  177: B<Inner and outer join types>
  178: 
  179:     my $inner = Database::Join->new(
  180:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  181:         join_column => 'entry',
  182:         join_type   => 'inner',   # only keys present in BOTH databases
  183:     );
  184: 
  185:     my $outer = Database::Join->new(
  186:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  187:         join_column => 'entry',
  188:         join_type   => 'outer',   # all keys from EITHER database
  189:     );
  190: 
  191: B<Building the view incrementally with add_database>
  192: 
  193:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  194:         databases   => [ $customers ],
  195:         join_column => 'entry',
  196:     );
  197: 
  198:     $join->add_database($loyalty)
  199:          ->add_database($scores, remove_columns => ['raw_score']);
  200: 
  201: B<AUTOLOAD column shortcut>
  202: 
  203:     # Returns the 'name' value for entry 'C001' (scalar context)
  204:     my $name = $join->name(entry => 'C001');
  205: 
  206:     # Returns all 'tier' values (list context)
  207:     my @tiers = $join->tier();
  208: 
  209: B<aggregate(): GROUP BY across the merged view>
  210: 
  211:     # Count and total score per tier, highest total first
  212:     my $rows = $join->aggregate(
  213:         by       => ['tier'],
  214:         measures => [
  215:             { col => 'score', fn => 'sum',   as => 'total' },
  216:             {                 fn => 'count', as => 'n'     },
  217:         ],
  218:         sort_by  => ['total', 'DESC'],
  219:         having   => { total => { '>' => 100 } },
  220:     );
  221:     # => [ { tier => 'gold', total => 183, n => 2 }, ... ]
  222: 
  223:     # Grand total across the whole view (no 'by' key)
  224:     my $summary = $join->aggregate(
  225:         measures => [{ fn => 'count', as => 'n' }],
  226:     );
  227:     # => [ { n => 42 } ]
  228: 
  229: B<SQLite join backend for large datasets>
  230: 
  231:     # 'auto' (default): switches to SQLite automatically above the threshold
  232:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  233:         databases      => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  234:         join_column    => 'entry',
  235:         backend        => 'auto',          # default
  236:         max_array_rows => 50_000,          # use SQLite when combined rows > 50,000
  237:     );
  238: 
  239:     # Always use SQLite -- useful when you know the data is large
  240:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  241:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  242:         join_column => 'entry',
  243:         backend     => 'sqlite',
  244:         tmpdir      => '/fast/nvme/tmp',   # optional: faster temp disk
  245:     );
  246: 
  247:     # Always use the original in-memory path
  248:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  249:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  250:         join_column => 'entry',
  251:         backend     => 'array',
  252:     );
  253: 
  254: =head1 DESCRIPTION
  255: 
  256: C<Database::Join> merges two or more L<Database::Abstraction> objects into a
  257: single logical, read-only view.  Each component database is queried
  258: independently through its own C<Database::Abstraction> interface.  The results
  259: are combined using a shared key column (C<join_column>).
  260: In effect, this means that you can view data from more than one database using
  261: an intuitive, non-SQL interface.
  262: 
  263: Every storage format that C<Database::Abstraction> supports works as a
  264: component database: CSV, PSV, TSV, SQLite, JSON, XML, XLSX, BerkeleyDB, HTML
  265: URL, JSON URL, or any custom subclass.  Component databases may mix formats
  266: within the same join.
  267: 
  268: The module exposes the same read-only API as C<Database::Abstraction>:
  269: C<selectall_arrayref>, C<selectall_array>, C<fetchrow_hashref>, C<count>,
  270: C<columns>, C<schema>, C<updated>, C<set_logger>, and the AUTOLOAD column
  271: shortcut.  Callers do not need to know how many underlying databases are
  272: involved.
  273: 
  274: Think of it as a virtual database table that is assembled on demand from
  275: several real tables, one per component database.
  276: 
  277: B<Join backends>
  278: 
  279: By default (C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'>), C<Database::Join> first checks the
  280: combined source row count.  For small datasets (up to C<max_array_rows>,
  281: default 10,000 rows) it merges entirely in Perl memory.  For larger datasets it
  282: automatically spills source rows into a temporary SQLite database and executes
  283: a single SQL JOIN there, keeping peak RAM to roughly one times the source data
  284: size instead of three.  You can also force either path unconditionally with
  285: C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'> or C<backend =E<gt> 'array'>.
  286: 
  287: =head2 Join semantics
  288: 
  289: The C<join_type> parameter controls what happens when a particular key value
  290: exists in some component databases but not all:
  291: 
  292: =over 4
  293: 
  294: =item C<left> (the default)
  295: 
  296: All rows from the I<primary> (first) database are returned.  Columns from
  297: subsequent databases are included where a matching row is found, and simply
  298: absent from the hashref where there is no match.  If you are familiar with
  299: SQL, this is a LEFT OUTER JOIN on the first table.
  300: 
  301: =item C<inner>
  302: 
  303: Only rows whose join-column value is present in I<every> component database
  304: are returned.  This is equivalent to a SQL INNER JOIN.
  305: 
  306: =item C<outer>
  307: 
  308: Every join-column value found in I<any> component database is returned.
  309: Columns from databases that do not have that key value are absent from the
  310: merged row.  This is a FULL OUTER JOIN.
  311: 
  312: =back
  313: 
  314: B<Important override rule:> whenever you pass a query criterion for a column
  315: that belongs to a secondary database, that database automatically acts as an
  316: inner-join partner for that query only -- regardless of C<join_type>.  This
  317: gives WHERE-clause semantics.  For example, if you have a LEFT join but query
  318: C<< tier => 'gold' >> on a secondary database, only rows whose secondary entry
  319: has tier = 'gold' are returned (rows with no secondary entry are excluded, just
  320: as a WHERE clause would exclude them).
  321: 
  322: =head2 Column ownership and routing
  323: 
  324: At construction time, C<Database::Join> calls C<columns()> on each component
  325: database and builds an internal index that maps every column name to the
  326: database that owns it.
  327: 
  328: When you pass criteria to a query method, each key-value pair is automatically
  329: routed to the right database.  You never need to say which database a column
  330: belongs to.
  331: 
  332: The C<join_column> is special: criteria on it are broadcast to I<all>
  333: databases so that each database fetches only the relevant rows before the
  334: in-memory merge.
  335: 
  336: When the same non-join column name exists in more than one database, the
  337: I<last> database in the C<databases> array wins by default: its value
  338: overwrites earlier ones in merged rows.  Use C<collision_prefix> to
  339: preserve both values under distinct names instead.
  340: 
  341: =head1 LIMITATIONS
  342: 
  343: =over 4
  344: 
  345: =item Memory usage (array backend)
  346: 
  347: When C<backend> is C<'array'> (or C<'auto'> and the dataset is small), all
  348: matching rows are fetched into Perl memory.  Peak RAM is roughly three times
  349: the source data size.  For large datasets use C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'>, or
  350: leave C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'> and set C<max_array_rows> appropriately.
  351: 
  352: =item SQLite backend uses a persistent cache file
  353: 
  354: When the SQLite path is active, a single C<.db> file with a randomly generated
  355: name (chosen by C<File::Temp> to avoid collisions) is created in C<tmpdir> the
  356: first time a query runs on a given C<Database::Join> object.  Source data is
  357: spilled into that file once; subsequent queries against the same object reuse
  358: the file without re-fetching the source data.
  359: 
  360: The cache is automatically invalidated and rebuilt whenever any source
  361: database's C<updated()> timestamp changes (indicating new data), or when
  362: C<add_database()> is called.
  363: 
  364: The file is deleted when the C<Database::Join> object is destroyed (typically
  365: when it goes out of scope).  At any given moment no more than one such file
  366: exists per object.  The directory must be writable and have enough free space
  367: for the full source data (once, not per-query).
  368: 
  369: =item No chained builder or raw SQL
  370: 
  371: C<query()> and C<execute()> are not implemented.  Use C<selectall_arrayref>
  372: or C<fetchrow_hashref> instead.
  373: 
  374: =item Single-column equi-join only
  375: 
  376: Joining on more than one column simultaneously, or on expressions, is not
  377: supported.  When the join key has different names in different databases,
  378: use C<join_map> to declare each database's local column name.
  379: 
  380: =item Sort order
  381: 
  382: Results are sorted ascending by C<join_column> by default.  Pass
  383: C<< sort_by => 'colname' >> (or C<< sort_by => ['colname', 'DESC'] >>)
  384: to any query method to override this.  The array path uses string comparison
  385: (C<cmp>); for accurate numeric ordering on large datasets use the SQLite
  386: backend, which sorts natively by type.
  387: 
  388: =item count() on the array backend fetches all rows
  389: 
  390: On the array backend, C<count()> executes the full in-memory join and counts
  391: the resulting rows in Perl; no C<COUNT(*)> is pushed to the component
  392: databases.  On the SQLite backend, C<count()> executes a C<SELECT COUNT(*)>
  393: SQL query against the cached join tables, avoiding a full row transfer.
  394: 
  395: =back
  396: 
  397: =head1 COMMON PITFALLS
  398: 
  399: =over 4
  400: 
  401: =item The join_column must exist in every component database
  402: 
  403: If even one database is missing the join key column, C<new()> (or
  404: C<add_database()>) will C<croak> immediately.  Use C<join_map> when the
  405: column has a different local name in some databases.
  406: 
  407: =item Criteria on a removed column are silently dropped
  408: 
  409: If you call C<remove_column('tier')> and later query
  410: C<< selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold') >>, the criterion is ignored (with a
  411: C<carp> warning) and all rows are returned.  Always pass criteria before
  412: removing columns, or restructure your code to avoid this.
  413: 
  414: =item You cannot remove the join column
  415: 
  416: C<< $join->remove_column($join->join_column) >> will C<croak>.  The join key
  417: is required for the merge to work.
  418: 
  419: =item Left join does not guarantee all columns are populated
  420: 
  421: Under a LEFT join, rows from the primary database that have no matching row
  422: in a secondary database will be returned with I<no keys> from that secondary
  423: database.  Accessing C<< $row->{score} >> on such a row returns C<undef> --
  424: not zero, not an empty string.  Always test C<defined $row->{score}> rather
  425: than just C<$row->{score}> when the secondary match is optional.
  426: 
  427: =item Filters act as inner-join partners
  428: 
  429: Any database that has a C<filters> entry is promoted to an inner-join partner,
  430: regardless of C<join_type>.  A row whose join-key value does not appear in
  431: the filtered database's result is removed from the merged output entirely, not
  432: merely missing its secondary columns.  This is intentional but can be
  433: surprising if you expected LEFT join semantics.
  434: 
  435: =item Criteria-merging replaces scalar filters
  436: 
  437: When both a base filter and a query criterion target the same column, and both
  438: are operator hashrefs (e.g. C<< { '>' => 60 } >>), the operators are combined
  439: (AND semantics).  But if the query criterion is a plain scalar (e.g.
  440: C<< score => 75 >>), it I<replaces> the base filter for that column entirely --
  441: the base filter is ignored for that query.
  442: 
  443: =item AUTOLOAD sees the full merged join when filters or join_map are active
  444: 
  445: When either C<filters> or C<join_map> is in effect, the AUTOLOAD shortcut
  446: (C<< $join->columnname(...) >>) runs the full join query rather than
  447: delegating directly to the owning database.  This is necessary for correctness
  448: but means the result respects all active filters and join-key translations,
  449: which may differ from what the owning database would return on its own.
  450: 
  451: =item Duplicate column names: last database wins (unless collision_prefix is set)
  452: 
  453: When two component databases each have a column called C<notes>, the second
  454: database's value silently overwrites the first in every merged row.  Use
  455: C<collision_prefix => { 1 => 'right' }> to publish the second database's
  456: C<notes> as C<right.notes> so both values survive, or use C<remove_columns>
  457: (or C<remove_column>) to drop the unwanted duplicate entirely.
  458: 
  459: =item Mutating the filters hashref after construction has no effect
  460: 
  461: C<Database::Join> deep-copies the C<filters> hashref (and any C<filter>
  462: passed to C<add_database>) at the moment of construction.  The original hashref
  463: you passed in is never stored.  If you later modify it -- for example, to
  464: tighten or loosen a filter criterion -- the joined view is I<not> affected.
  465: Construct a new C<Database::Join> object, or use a component
  466: C<Database::Abstraction> that supports dynamic filter modification.
  467: 
  468: =item auto mode may always use the array path for some DAs
  469: 
  470: C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'> counts rows cheaply only when each component database
  471: either implements C<dbi_source()> (SQLite-backed) or directly defines a
  472: C<count()> method in its own package.  A DA that merely I<inherits> C<count()>
  473: from C<Database::Abstraction> is treated as uncountable, because the parent
  474: class C<count()> expects a key argument and behaves differently from a
  475: "return total row count" function.  In that case C<Database::Join>
  476: conservatively uses the array path for the whole query, even if the dataset is
  477: large.  To opt in to the SQLite path for such a DA, either add your own
  478: C<count()> override that returns the total row count, implement C<dbi_source()>,
  479: or use C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'> unconditionally.
  480: 
  481: =item dbi_source() ATTACH is unconditional - query-time criteria go into WHERE
  482: 
  483: When a component database implements C<dbi_source()>, C<Database::Join> always
  484: uses the zero-copy ATTACH path, I<even when the current query includes criteria
  485: for columns in that database>.  The criteria are translated into parameterised
  486: SQL C<WHERE> clauses applied against the ATTACHed table; no row-level copy is
  487: performed.  (Prior to 0.005.0 the presence of any query-time criteria would
  488: force a spill; that restriction has been removed.)
  489: 
  490: =item Broadcast join-column criterion does not force secondaries into inner-join
  491: 
  492: When a caller passes a join-column criterion (e.g. C<entry =E<gt> 'k1'>),
  493: C<Database::Join> broadcasts it to all component databases so each DA can
  494: filter its fetch to the requested key.  Prior to 0.006.0 this broadcast was
  495: incorrectly counted as "having criteria" for secondary databases, causing
  496: C<left> and C<outer> joins to silently behave as C<inner> joins when a
  497: join-column criterion was present.  The fix: only non-join-column criteria
  498: (e.g. column filters from the caller or base C<filters =E<gt> {...}>) promote
  499: a secondary to inner-join status.  The broadcast itself is now a transparent
  500: key-range selector that does not affect join semantics.
  501: 
  502: =item LIKE and NOT LIKE work on the SQLite path; other pattern operators do not yet
  503: 
  504: C<LIKE>, C<NOT LIKE>, C<IN>, and C<NOT IN> are fully supported on the SQLite
  505: backend.  C<LIKE>/C<NOT LIKE> take a scalar pattern; C<IN>/C<NOT IN> take an
  506: arrayref of values.  All are injection-safe because values are passed as bind
  507: parameters, never interpolated.
  508: 
  509:     # LIKE
  510:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(name => { LIKE => 'A%' });
  511: 
  512:     # IN
  513:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => { IN => ['gold', 'silver'] });
  514: 
  515:     # NOT IN
  516:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => { 'NOT IN' => ['bronze'] });
  517: 
  518: C<IN> with an empty arrayref matches no rows (SQL semantics: C<IN ()> is
  519: always false).  C<NOT IN> with an empty arrayref matches all rows (no
  520: constraint added).
  521: 
  522: C<IS NULL> and C<IS NOT NULL> are supported on the SQLite backend using an
  523: explicit operator hashref:
  524: 
  525:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(score => { 'IS NULL'     => undef });
  526:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(score => { 'IS NOT NULL' => 1    });
  527: 
  528: The hashref value is ignored; only the key selects the operator.  A bare
  529: C<undef> criterion value (C<< score => undef >>) also generates C<IS NULL>
  530: on the SQLite path.  Note: on the in-memory array path, the component DA
  531: may treat an C<undef> criterion value as C<< no filter >> rather than
  532: C<IS NULL>, so use the explicit hashref form for consistent behaviour
  533: across backends.
  534: 
  535: Any operator not in the supported set is skipped on the SQLite path with a
  536: C<carp> warning.  The array path forwards all operators to the component DA
  537: unchanged, which may or may not honour them.
  538: 
  539: =item Temp file directory must be writable and have free space
  540: 
  541: The SQLite path creates one temporary C<.db> file per C<Database::Join> object
  542: in C<tmpdir> (default: C<File::Spec-E<gt>tmpdir()>, usually C</tmp> on Unix).
  543: The filename is randomly generated by C<File::Temp> -- you cannot predict it,
  544: only the directory is under your control.  The file is created on the first
  545: query and kept alive until the object is destroyed; it is not re-created on
  546: every query call.  If the directory is not writable, or the filesystem is full,
  547: the call will C<croak> with C<error_sqlite_connect>.  Check permissions and
  548: free space if you see that error.
  549: 
  550: =item C<join =E<gt>> criteria are not supported
  551: 
  552: C<Database::Abstraction> accepts a C<join =E<gt> { table =E<gt> ..., on =E<gt> ... }>
  553: key in its criteria hashrefs to express an SQL JOIN within a single table.
  554: C<Database::Join> cannot route this to a component DA meaningfully: the merged
  555: view has no concept of a single underlying table.  Passing C<join =E<gt>> to any
  556: query method will C<croak> with C<error_join_criterion>.
  557: 
  558: B<Fix:> Model the joined table as a separate C<Database::Abstraction> object and
  559: add it to the C<databases =E<gt> []> list.  Use C<join_column> or C<join_map> to
  560: specify the shared key.
  561: 
  562: =item aggregate(): omitting C<by> gives a grand total, not an error
  563: 
  564: Calling C<aggregate()> without a C<by> key (or with C<< by => [] >>) does
  565: I<not> croak.  It returns a single-element arrayref containing one row with
  566: all the computed measure aliases.  This is a grand total over the entire view
  567: (possibly filtered by any extra criteria you pass).
  568: 
  569:     my $total = $join->aggregate(measures => [{ fn => 'count', as => 'n' }]);
  570:     # $total->[0]{n} is the total row count
  571: 
  572: If you expected multiple rows and got one, you probably forgot the C<by> key.
  573: 
  574: =item aggregate(): non-numeric values behave differently across backends
  575: 
  576: On the array backend (C<backend =E<gt> 'array'>), any non-numeric value in a
  577: column targeted by C<sum> or C<avg> is I<skipped> (not counted, not coerced),
  578: and a C<carp> warning C<warn_aggregate_non_numeric> is emitted.  On the SQLite
  579: backend, non-numeric values are silently coerced to C<0.0> via SQL C<CAST>.
  580: The two backends therefore produce different numeric totals when the data
  581: contains non-numeric values.  Clean your data first if you need consistent
  582: results across backends, or choose a backend explicitly.
  583: 
  584: =item aggregate(): C<BETWEEN> is not supported in C<having>
  585: 
  586: The C<having> hashref uses the same operator vocabulary as C<selectall_arrayref>,
  587: which does not include C<BETWEEN>.  Write a range as two explicit constraints:
  588: 
  589:     # Correct
  590:     having => { total => { '>=' => 10, '<=' => 50 } }
  591: 
  592:     # Wrong -- will croak with error_aggregate_having_operator
  593:     having => { total => { BETWEEN => [10, 50] } }
  594: 
  595: =item aggregate(): C<count> without C<col> counts all rows, not distinct non-NULL values
  596: 
  597: A measure with C<< fn => 'count' >> and no C<col> key emits C<COUNT(*)> on the
  598: SQLite backend (or counts every row in Perl).  This counts rows that have
  599: C<undef>/NULL in other columns too.  To count only rows where a specific
  600: column is non-NULL, or to count distinct values, use C<count_distinct>:
  601: 
  602:     # Count rows with a non-NULL 'score' (one way -- use a pre-filter)
  603:     measures => [{ col => 'score', fn => 'count_distinct', as => 'distinct_scores' }]
  604: 
  605: =item aggregate(): the result is never cached
  606: 
  607: Unlike C<selectall_arrayref> (which benefits from the SQLite file cache on
  608: repeated identical calls), C<aggregate()> always re-executes.  If your
  609: aggregation is expensive and the data changes infrequently, cache the result
  610: yourself using C<updated()> as a staleness signal.
  611: 
  612: =item distinct => 1 without columns returns full rows, not a column projection
  613: 
  614: C<< distinct => 1 >> alone deduplicates on I<all> columns of the merged row.
  615: If you want a distinct list of values for a single column (e.g. all unique
  616: tiers), you B<must> supply C<< columns => ['tier'] >> as well.  Without
  617: C<columns>, every column participates in the key used to detect duplicates,
  618: which means two rows that differ in any column (e.g. C<entry>) are never
  619: considered duplicates, and the result will be the same as without C<distinct>.
  620: 
  621: =item distinct => 1 with columns projects rows to those columns
  622: 
  623: When C<columns> is supplied alongside C<distinct>, each returned row contains
  624: I<only> the named columns -- all other columns are dropped.  The projection
  625: happens before deduplication, so the result set may be smaller than the full
  626: merged row count.  If you need the full row I<and> want to know unique values,
  627: run two separate queries or post-process in Perl.
  628: 
  629: =item mandatory_filters must never come from user-supplied input
  630: 
  631: The predicates in C<mandatory_filters> form an enforcement boundary.  They must
  632: come from a trusted server-side source (a session store, a policy table, or
  633: hard-coded application configuration) -- never from URL parameters, form fields,
  634: HTTP headers, or other user-controlled input.  A hostile user who can control
  635: a mandatory_filters predicate can widen it to C<{ col => 'status', op => 'ne',
  636: val => 'NONEXISTENT' }> and receive all rows regardless of status.
  637: 
  638: =item mandatory_filters uses string comparison on the array path
  639: 
  640: On the array backend, all C<mandatory_filters> comparisons (including C<lt>,
  641: C<lte>, C<gt>, C<gte>) are I<lexicographic string comparisons>, not numeric.
  642: C<< { col => 'score', op => 'gt', val => 9 } >> will match a score of C<'10'>
  643: only if C<'10' gt '9'> is true -- which it is not (C<'1' lt '9'>).  Use the
  644: SQLite backend (C<< backend => 'sqlite' >>) when numeric comparisons are needed,
  645: as SQLite uses the column's declared type for ordering.
  646: 
  647: =back
  648: 
  649: =head1 METHODS
  650: 
  651: =head2 new
  652: 
  653: =head3 SYNOPSIS
  654: 
  655:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  656:         databases         => [ $db1, $db2 ],
  657:         join_column       => 'entry',
  658:         join_type         => 'left',
  659:         join_map          => { 1 => 'local_col' },
  660:         filters           => { 1 => { score => { '>' => 60 } } },
  661:         base_criteria     => { active => 1 },
  662:         mandatory_filters => [{ col => 'region', op => 'eq', val => 'West' }],
  663:         collision_prefix  => { 1 => 'right' },
  664:         remove_columns    => [ 'email', 'internal_id' ],
  665:         backend           => 'auto',        # 'auto' | 'sqlite' | 'array'
  666:         max_array_rows    => 10_000,        # threshold for 'auto' mode
  667:         tmpdir            => '/tmp',        # directory for temp SQLite file
  668:         logger            => $log,
  669:         i18n              => $locale,
  670:     );
  671: 
  672: =head3 DESCRIPTION
  673: 
  674: Constructs and returns a new C<Database::Join> object.
  675: 
  676: Each element of C<databases> must be an already-instantiated subclass of
  677: C<Database::Abstraction>.  The constructor calls C<columns()> on every
  678: database to build an internal column-routing table and verifies that
  679: C<join_column> (or its local alias from C<join_map>) is present in each one.
  680: 
  681: Columns listed in C<remove_columns> are hidden immediately: they do not appear
  682: in C<columns()>, C<schema()>, or any returned row hashref.  This is equivalent
  683: to calling C<remove_column> once per name after construction.
  684: 
  685: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
  686: 
  687: =head4 Input
  688: 
  689:     databases      => { type => 'arrayref', required => 1 }
  690:                       # One or more Database::Abstraction subclass objects.
  691:                       #
  692:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   non-empty arrayref of blessed DA subclasses.
  693:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: scalar, hashref, or absent => croak.
  694:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA size:   minimum 1 element; no documented upper bound.
  695:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA elem:   each element must pass isa('Database::Abstraction').
  696: 
  697:     join_column    => { type => 'string',   optional => 1, default => 'entry' }
  698:                       # The column name shared by all databases (the join key).
  699:                       #
  700:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any non-empty string present in every component DA.
  701:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: column absent from any DA => croak join_col_missing.
  702:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        empty string '' is treated as a column name and
  703:                       #                       will croak if (as expected) it is absent from every DA.
  704:                       # DOMAIN -- NOTE:       matching is case-sensitive and exact.
  705: 
  706:     join_type      => { type => 'string',   optional => 1, default => 'left',
  707:                         enum => ['inner', 'left', 'outer'] }
  708:                       # Controls which keys appear in the result when not all
  709:                       # databases share the same key values.
  710:                       #
  711:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   exactly 'inner', 'left', or 'outer'.
  712:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: any other string including 'INNER', 'LEFT',
  713:                       #                       'OUTER' (enum check is case-sensitive), 'cross',
  714:                       #                       or '' => croak from validate_strict.
  715: 
  716:     join_map       => { type => 'hashref',  optional => 1 }
  717:                       # Zero-based database index => local column name.
  718:                       # See the join_map section for full details.
  719:                       #
  720:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   hashref values must be plain strings.
  721:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: reference value (hashref, arrayref, coderef, etc.)
  722:                       #                       => croak; the guard prevents heap-address leakage.
  723:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        out-of-range keys (beyond the databases array) are
  724:                       #                       silently ignored.
  725: 
  726:     filters        => { type => 'hashref',  optional => 1 }
  727:                       # Zero-based database index => criteria hashref.
  728:                       # Permanent row restrictions on individual databases.
  729:                       # See the filters section for full details.
  730: 
  731:     base_criteria  => { type => 'hashref',  optional => 1 }
  732:                       # Column name => value hashref.
  733:                       # Permanent criteria applied to every query, specified by
  734:                       # column name rather than database index.  Each key must
  735:                       # be a column that appears in the merged view (or the
  736:                       # join_column); each value is a plain scalar or an
  737:                       # operator hashref in the same format as
  738:                       # selectall_arrayref accepts.  Criteria are automatically
  739:                       # routed to the database that owns each column (same
  740:                       # routing used by selectall_arrayref).
  741:                       # Equivalent to filters but more convenient when you know
  742:                       # the column name but not which database index owns it.
  743:                       # When a column appears in both base_criteria and filters,
  744:                       # the filters entry takes precedence.
  745:                       # See the base_criteria section for full details.
  746:                       #
  747:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   hashref of column-name => scalar or
  748:                       #                       operator-hashref pairs.  Unknown column
  749:                       #                       names emit warn_unknown_column (carp)
  750:                       #                       and are silently dropped.
  751:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: non-hashref value => croak from
  752:                       #                       validate_strict.
  753:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        {} empty hashref is a safe no-op.
  754: 
  755:     collision_prefix => { type => 'hashref', optional => 1 }
  756:                       # Zero-based database index (>0) => prefix string.
  757:                       # When a secondary database has a column that collides
  758:                       # with a column already present in the merged view, the
  759:                       # secondary column is published as "$prefix.$col" instead
  760:                       # of silently overwriting the earlier value.
  761:                       # Index 0 entries are silently ignored.
  762:                       # Omitting this parameter preserves the original
  763:                       # last-database-wins behaviour.
  764:                       # See the collision_prefix section for full details.
  765:                       #
  766:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   absent or {} => last-database-wins (no change).
  767:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   { N => 'prefix' } where N > 0 => colliding
  768:                       #                       columns from DB[N] published as "$prefix.$col";
  769:                       #                       non-colliding columns from the same DB added plain.
  770:                       # DOMAIN -- EP note:    index-0 entries are silently ignored.
  771:                       # DOMAIN -- Invariant:  join_column is never prefixed regardless of
  772:                       #                       collision_prefix configuration.
  773: 
  774:     remove_columns => { type => 'arrayref', optional => 1 }
  775:                       # Column names to hide from the merged view.
  776:                       #
  777:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   arrayref of any strings; non-existent columns
  778:                       #                       are silently ignored (idempotent).
  779:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: join_column itself => croak remove_join_col.
  780:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        [] empty arrayref is a safe no-op.
  781: 
  782:     backend        => { type => 'string',   optional => 1, default => 'auto',
  783:                         enum => ['array', 'sqlite', 'auto'] }
  784:                       # Controls which join strategy is used.
  785:                       #   'auto'   -- (default) use 'array' when combined source row count
  786:                       #              <= max_array_rows, 'sqlite' otherwise.
  787:                       #   'sqlite' -- always spill to a temporary SQLite database.
  788:                       #   'array'  -- always use the in-memory merge path.
  789:                       #
  790:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   'array', 'sqlite', or 'auto' (case-sensitive).
  791:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: any other string => croak error_invalid_backend.
  792:                       # DOMAIN -- Default:    'auto'.
  793: 
  794:     max_array_rows => { type => 'integer',  optional => 1, default => 10_000 }
  795:                       # Row-count threshold for 'auto' mode.  When the combined
  796:                       # source row count exceeds this value, the SQLite path is used.
  797:                       # Ignored when backend is 'array' or 'sqlite'.
  798:                       #
  799:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any non-negative integer.
  800:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        0 means always use SQLite (all counts exceed 0).
  801:                       # DOMAIN -- Default:    10,000.
  802: 
  803:     tmpdir         => { type => 'string',   optional => 1 }
  804:                       # Directory for the per-call temporary SQLite database file.
  805:                       # The file is created securely by File::Temp and removed when
  806:                       # the query completes.  Ignored when backend is 'array'.
  807:                       #
  808:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any writable directory path string.
  809:                       # DOMAIN -- EP absent:  uses File::Spec->tmpdir() (system temp dir).
  810: 
  811:     parallel       => { type => 'integer',  optional => 1, default => 0 }
  812:                       # When set to 1 and the join has more than 2 databases (primary +
  813:                       # 2 or more secondaries), secondary DA fetches are issued in
  814:                       # parallel Perl threads.  Requires the 'threads' module; falls
  815:                       # back to sequential with a carp warning when unavailable.
  816:                       # Has no effect on the SQLite backend (which uses a single SQL
  817:                       # JOIN).  DBI-backed DAs are not thread-safe by default; only
  818:                       # enable this for in-memory or otherwise thread-safe DA backends.
  819:                       #
  820:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   0 (sequential, default) or 1 (parallel).
  821:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: any other integer is treated as truthy/falsy.
  822:                       # DOMAIN -- Default:    0.
  823: 
  824:     autoload_pk     => { type => 'string',   optional => 1 }
  825:                       # The column name used as the positional-argument key when
  826:                       # AUTOLOAD performs a full join query (i.e. when join_map or
  827:                       # filters is active).  Calling $join->col('value') with a bare
  828:                       # scalar maps that scalar to { autoload_pk => 'value' } before
  829:                       # routing through the merged view.
  830:                       #
  831:                       # Defaults to join_column when omitted.  Override when the
  832:                       # primary DA's natural lookup key differs from join_column -
  833:                       # for example, when join_column is 'statecode' but you want to
  834:                       # look up by city name ('entry').
  835:                       #
  836:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any string that is a visible column in
  837:                       #                       the merged view (checked at call time by
  838:                       #                       _partition_criteria; unknown names are
  839:                       #                       carp'd and dropped).
  840:                       # DOMAIN -- EP absent:  defaults to join_column.
  841: 
  842:     mandatory_filters => { type => 'arrayref', optional => 1 }
  843:                       # Arrayref of {col, op, val} predicate hashrefs.
  844:                       # These filters are applied to every query result regardless
  845:                       # of what criteria the caller supplies.  They cannot be
  846:                       # overridden, suppressed, or circumvented at query time.
  847:                       # Each predicate must supply:
  848:                       #   col -- a column name present in the merged view
  849:                       #   op  -- one of: eq, ne, lt, lte, gt, gte, like, contains
  850:                       #   val -- the comparison value (plain scalar)
  851:                       # On the array backend: applied as a Perl-side grep after the
  852:                       # in-memory merge, before sort/limit/offset.
  853:                       # On the SQLite backend: injected as parameterised WHERE clauses
  854:                       # into the per-call SQL query.
  855:                       #
  856:                       # SECURITY: Never set this from URL parameters or any
  857:                       # user-supplied value.  Predicates must come from the
  858:                       # server-side session or policy store only.
  859:                       #
  860:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   [] empty arrayref => no constraint (safe no-op).
  861:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   arrayref of {col, op, val} hashrefs where
  862:                       #                       col is in the merged view and op is supported.
  863:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: element is not a hashref, or is missing
  864:                       #                       col/op/val => croak error_mandatory_filter_ref.
  865:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: col not in merged view => croak error_mandatory_filter_col.
  866:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: op not in supported set => croak error_mandatory_filter_op.
  867:                       # DOMAIN -- ops:        eq (string eq), ne (string ne),
  868:                       #                       lt/lte/gt/gte (string comparison),
  869:                       #                       like (SQL LIKE pattern with % and _),
  870:                       #                       contains (substring match, equivalent to LIKE %val%).
  871: 
  872:     logger         => { type => 'object',   optional => 1 }
  873:                       # Logger object propagated to all component databases.
  874: 
  875:     i18n           => { type => 'object',   optional => 1 }
  876:                       # Localisation object with a translate($key, @args) method.
  877: 
  878: =head4 Output
  879: 
  880:     A blessed Database::Join object.
  881: 
  882: =head3 EXAMPLE
  883: 
  884:     # Customers database: entry | name | email
  885:     # Loyalty   database: entry | tier | points
  886: 
  887:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  888:         databases      => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  889:         join_column    => 'entry',
  890:         join_type      => 'inner',            # only customers who also have loyalty records
  891:         remove_columns => [ 'email' ],        # hide PII from query results
  892:         filters        => { 1 => { points => { '>' => 0 } } }, # ignore zero-point records
  893:     );
  894: 
  895:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
  896:     # Each row: { entry => ..., name => ..., tier => ..., points => ... }
  897:     # 'email' is absent. Zero-point loyalty records are excluded.
  898: 
  899: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
  900: 
  901:     validate all parameters with validate_strict
  902:     croak if databases is empty
  903:     croak if any element of databases is not a Database::Abstraction subclass
  904:     bless the object with all fields initialised
  905:     call _build_col_index to map every column to its owning database
  906:         and verify join_column presence in each database
  907:     if base_criteria given:
  908:         partition base_criteria by column ownership into per-db slices
  909:         for each database with a non-empty slice:
  910:             merge the slice into _filters[i]
  911:             (explicit filters win on plain-scalar conflicts)
  912:     if mandatory_filters given:
  913:         for each predicate in mandatory_filters:
  914:             croak error_mandatory_filter_ref if predicate is not a {col, op, val} hashref
  915:             croak error_mandatory_filter_col if col is not in the merged view
  916:             croak error_mandatory_filter_op  if op is not in the supported set
  917:         store shallow copies of all validated predicates in _mandatory_filters
  918:     for each column in remove_columns: call remove_column
  919:     return the new object
  920: 
  921: =head3 MESSAGES
  922: 
  923:     error_no_databases           -- databases arrayref was empty
  924:     error_invalid_db             -- an element of databases is not a D::A subclass
  925:     error_join_col_missing       -- join_column (or its join_map alias) not found in a database
  926:     error_invalid_backend        -- backend value is not 'array', 'sqlite', or 'auto'
  927:     error_sqlite_connect         -- temporary SQLite database could not be created (backend='sqlite'/'auto')

  930:     error_mandatory_filter_op    -- a mandatory_filters element uses an unsupported op value
  931:     warn_schema_type_mismatch    -- (carp) a shared column has different types across databases;
  932:                                     use collision_prefix to preserve both values
  933: 
  934: =cut
