 1082: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 1083: # Public API (mirrors Database::Abstraction)
 1084: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 1085: 
 1086: =head2 join_map - joining on differently-named columns
 1087: 
 1088: By default every component database must have a column whose name matches
 1089: C<join_column>.  If a database uses a different local name for the join key,
 1090: declare the mapping with C<join_map>.
 1091: 
 1092: C<join_map> is a hashref.  Each B<key> is the B<zero-based position> of a
 1093: database in the C<databases> array (0 = first, 1 = second, and so on).  Each
 1094: B<value> is the name that B<that particular database> uses for the join key.
 1095: 
 1096: Databases not listed in C<join_map> are assumed to already have a column
 1097: named C<join_column> and need no entry.
 1098: 
 1099: Throughout the merged view the join key is I<always> referred to by the name
 1100: given in C<join_column>.  The local alias is never exposed in returned rows,
 1101: in C<columns()>, or in C<schema()>.
 1102: 
 1103: B<When do you need join_map?>
 1104: 
 1105: You need C<join_map> when you have two tables like:
 1106: 
 1107:     cities table  : entry (the city name) | statecode
 1108:     stnames table : entry (the state code) | state
 1109: 
 1110: Here you want to join cities.statecode to stnames.entry.  You choose
 1111: C<< join_column => 'statecode' >> as the canonical name, but stnames calls
 1112: that same concept C<entry>, so you declare:
 1113: 
 1114:     join_map => { 1 => 'entry' }  # stnames (index 1) calls it 'entry'
 1115: 
 1116: B<Example>
 1117: 
 1118:     #                        index 0     index 1
 1119:     my @databases = (       $cities,    $stnames  );
 1120:     #  join key column:    'statecode'  'entry'
 1121:     #  join_column:        'statecode' (chosen canonical name)
 1122:     #  stnames differs, so declare the alias:
 1123: 
 1124:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1125:         databases   => \@databases,
 1126:         join_column => 'statecode',
 1127:         join_map    => { 1 => 'entry' },
 1128:     );
 1129: 
 1130:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1131:     # Each $row has keys: entry (city), statecode, state
 1132:     # 'entry' from stnames is never exposed directly.
 1133: 
 1134:     my $row = $join->fetchrow_hashref(statecode => 'CA');
 1135: 
 1136: B<Using add_database instead>
 1137: 
 1138: If you build the join incrementally with C<add_database>, pass
 1139: C<join_column> directly to that call instead of using C<join_map>:
 1140: 
 1141:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1142:         databases   => [ $cities ],
 1143:         join_column => 'statecode',
 1144:     );
 1145:     $join->add_database($stnames, join_column => 'entry');
 1146: 
 1147: This is exactly equivalent to the C<join_map> form above.
 1148: 
 1149: =head2 filters - permanent per-database row filters
 1150: 
 1151: C<filters> lets you restrict a component database to a subset of its rows
 1152: permanently, without repeating the criterion on every query call.
 1153: 
 1154: Think of it as telling the join: "whenever you query this database, always
 1155: add these extra conditions".  Callers never need to specify the restriction
 1156: themselves and can never accidentally omit it.
 1157: 
 1158: C<filters> is a hashref.  Each B<key> is the B<zero-based position> of a
 1159: database in the C<databases> array (same numbering as C<join_map>).  Each
 1160: B<value> is a criteria hashref in the same format as C<selectall_arrayref>
 1161: accepts.
 1162: 
 1163: B<Key-set semantics>
 1164: 
 1165: A filtered database always acts as an inner-join partner, regardless of the
 1166: C<join_type> setting.  Any join-key value that does not pass the filter is
 1167: excluded from the merged output entirely -- not just missing its secondary
 1168: columns.  This ensures the filter genuinely restricts the view rather than
 1169: simply hiding a few fields.
 1170: 
 1171: B<Criteria merging>
 1172: 
 1173: When a query call also passes a criterion for a column that already has a base
 1174: filter, the two constraints are combined:
 1175: 
 1176: =over 4
 1177: 
 1178: =item *
 1179: 
 1180: When both the base filter value and the query criterion are operator hashrefs
 1181: (e.g. C<< { '>' => 60 } >> and C<< { '<' => 365 } >>), their operators are
 1182: merged: I<both> constraints apply simultaneously (AND semantics).
 1183: 
 1184: =item *
 1185: 
 1186: When either value is a plain scalar, or the operators conflict, the
 1187: query-time criterion wins and the base filter for that column is ignored for
 1188: that one call.
 1189: 
 1190: =back
 1191: 
 1192: B<Example -- only show orders placed more than 60 days ago>
 1193: 
 1194:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1195:         databases   => [ $customers, $orders ],
 1196:         join_column => 'entry',
 1197:         filters     => { 1 => { age_days => { '>' => 60 } } },
 1198:     );
 1199: 
 1200:     # Every query automatically sees only old orders
 1201:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1202: 
 1203:     # Additional criteria layer on top -- gold tier AND old order
 1204:     my $vip  = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');
 1205: 
 1206:     # Range intersection: age_days > 60 AND age_days < 365
 1207:     my $mid  = $join->selectall_arrayref(age_days => { '<' => 365 });
 1208: 
 1209: When using C<add_database>, pass C<filter> (singular) to set the base
 1210: criteria for the new database:
 1211: 
 1212:     $join->add_database($orders, filter => { age_days => { '>' => 60 } });
 1213: 
 1214: =head2 base_criteria - permanent view-level row filters by column name
 1215: 
 1216: C<base_criteria> is a convenience alternative to C<filters> for callers who
 1217: know the column names they want to restrict but prefer not to track database
 1218: indices.
 1219: 
 1220:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1221:         databases     => [ $customers, $orders ],
 1222:         join_column   => 'entry',
 1223:         base_criteria => { active => 1, deleted_at => undef },
 1224:     );
 1225: 
 1226:     # Every query automatically sees only active, non-deleted rows.
 1227:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1228: 
 1229: At construction time C<base_criteria> is partitioned by column ownership
 1230: using the same routing logic as C<selectall_arrayref>.  Each criterion is
 1231: sent to the database that owns that column and stored as a permanent base
 1232: filter (equivalent to the corresponding C<filters> entry).
 1233: 
 1234: B<Unknown columns> emit a C<warn_unknown_column> carp and are silently
 1235: dropped, just as they would be in a query call.
 1236: 
 1237: B<Key-set semantics> are identical to C<filters>: any database that receives
 1238: a C<base_criteria> slice acts as an inner-join partner regardless of
 1239: C<join_type>.
 1240: 
 1241: B<Precedence>: when a column appears in both C<base_criteria> and C<filters>,
 1242: the C<filters> entry wins on plain-scalar conflicts; operator-hashref values
 1243: are combined with AND semantics.
 1244: 
 1245: B<Use cases>
 1246: 
 1247: =over 4
 1248: 
 1249: =item *
 1250: 
 1251: Row-level security: C<< base_criteria => { tenant_id => $tid } >>
 1252: 
 1253: =item *
 1254: 
 1255: Soft-delete filtering: C<< base_criteria => { deleted_at => undef } >>
 1256: 
 1257: =item *
 1258: 
 1259: Status gates: C<< base_criteria => { active => 1 } >>
 1260: 
 1261: =back
 1262: 
 1263: =head2 mandatory_filters - row-level security predicates that cannot be bypassed
 1264: 
 1265: C<mandatory_filters> is an arrayref of C<{col, op, val}> predicate hashrefs.
 1266: Every predicate is applied to the merged result of every query, regardless of
 1267: what criteria the caller passes.  Unlike C<filters> and C<base_criteria> (which
 1268: are per-database constraints applied at fetch time), C<mandatory_filters>
 1269: operates on the fully-merged row set, ensuring the constraint applies uniformly
 1270: even when the same logical value is spread across multiple databases.
 1271: 
 1272:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1273:         databases         => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
 1274:         join_column       => 'entry',
 1275:         mandatory_filters => [
 1276:             { col => 'region',  op => 'eq',  val => 'West' },
 1277:             { col => 'tier',    op => 'ne',  val => 'banned' },
 1278:             { col => 'balance', op => 'gte', val => 0        },
 1279:         ],
 1280:     );
 1281: 
 1282: Each predicate hashref must supply exactly these three keys:
 1283: 
 1284: =over 4
 1285: 
 1286: =item C<col>
 1287: 
 1288: The column name in the merged view (as returned by C<columns()>).  Must be
 1289: present at construction time; columns added later via C<add_database()> are not
 1290: retroactively covered by predicates already in C<_mandatory_filters>.
 1291: 
 1292: =item C<op>
 1293: 
 1294: The comparison operator.  Supported values:
 1295: 
 1296:     eq       -- string equality      ($row->{col} eq $val)
 1297:     ne       -- string inequality    ($row->{col} ne $val)
 1298:     lt       -- string less-than     ($row->{col} lt $val)
 1299:     lte      -- string <=            ($row->{col} le $val)
 1300:     gt       -- string greater-than  ($row->{col} gt $val)
 1301:     gte      -- string >=            ($row->{col} ge $val)
 1302:     like     -- SQL LIKE pattern     (% = any sequence, _ = any one character)
 1303:     contains -- substring match      (row value must contain val as a substring)
 1304: 
 1305: Comparisons are lexicographic (string-based) on the array path; on the SQLite
 1306: path they follow SQLite's default collation for the column type.  Use the
 1307: SQLite backend (C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'>) when numeric ordering matters.
 1308: 
 1309: =item C<val>
 1310: 
 1311: The right-hand side of the comparison.  Must be a plain scalar.
 1312: 
 1313: =back
 1314: 
 1315: B<Supported use cases>
 1316: 
 1317: =over 4
 1318: 
 1319: =item *
 1320: 
 1321: Row-level security: restrict the view to rows the current session is authorised
 1322: to see: C<< { col => 'tenant_id', op => 'eq', val => $session_tenant } >>
 1323: 
 1324: =item *
 1325: 
 1326: Data classification gates: hide rows marked with a sensitivity label:
 1327: C<< { col => 'classification', op => 'ne', val => 'restricted' } >>
 1328: 
 1329: =item *
 1330: 
 1331: Substring/pattern guards:
 1332: C<< { col => 'email', op => 'contains', val => '@example.com' } >>
 1333: or C<< { col => 'code', op => 'like', val => 'PROD_%' } >>
 1334: 
 1335: =back
 1336: 
 1337: B<SECURITY: C<mandatory_filters> must never be set from user-supplied values>
 1338: 
 1339: The predicates in C<mandatory_filters> are an enforcement boundary.  They must
 1340: come from a trusted server-side source (a session store, a policy table, or
 1341: hard-coded application configuration) -- never from URL parameters, form fields,
 1342: HTTP headers, or any other user-controlled input.  Setting this parameter from
 1343: user-supplied data would allow an attacker to widen or replace the constraints
 1344: and access rows they are not permitted to see.
 1345: 
 1346: B<Array vs. SQLite backend>
 1347: 
 1348: On the B<array backend>, C<mandatory_filters> is applied as a Perl-side C<grep>
 1349: after the in-memory merge, immediately before C<sort_by>/C<limit>/C<offset>.
 1350: A row that does not satisfy every predicate is silently dropped.
 1351: 
 1352: On the B<SQLite backend>, each predicate is translated into a parameterised
 1353: C<WHERE> clause and injected into the per-call SQL query.  Values are always
 1354: passed via DBI bind parameters, never by string interpolation.
 1355: 
 1356: Both backends produce identical filtered results.
 1357: 
 1358: =head2 distinct - deduplicate merged rows
 1359: 
 1360: Pass C<< distinct => 1 >> to C<selectall_arrayref> or C<selectall_array> to
 1361: remove duplicate rows from the merged result.
 1362: 
 1363:     # All unique tier values
 1364:     my $tiers = $join->selectall_arrayref(
 1365:         distinct => 1,
 1366:         columns  => ['tier'],
 1367:     );
 1368: 
 1369:     # All unique region + tier combinations
 1370:     my $combos = $join->selectall_arrayref(
 1371:         distinct => 1,
 1372:         columns  => ['region', 'tier'],
 1373:     );
 1374: 
 1375:     # Remove fully-identical merged rows (all columns participate)
 1376:     my $deduped = $join->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1);
 1377: 
 1378: The optional C<columns> arrayref projects each row to the named columns I<before>
 1379: deduplication.  Omitting C<columns> (or passing an empty arrayref) deduplicates
 1380: on the full merged row: two rows must be identical in every column to be
 1381: considered duplicates.
 1382: 
 1383: B<Array backend>: deduplication is done in Perl using a seen-hash keyed on the
 1384: selected column values.  First occurrence wins.
 1385: 
 1386: B<SQLite backend>: C<SELECT DISTINCT col1, col2, ...> is used; the SQL engine
 1387: handles deduplication.
 1388: 
 1389: Both backends produce identical results.
 1390: 
 1391: B<Interaction with sort_by / limit / offset>: C<distinct> is applied first
 1392: (before pagination and ordering), so C<limit> and C<offset> operate on the
 1393: already-deduplicated set.
 1394: 
 1395: =head2 collision_prefix - preserve colliding columns from secondary databases
 1396: 
 1397: By default, when a column name appears in more than one database the I<last>
 1398: database wins: its value silently overwrites earlier ones in merged rows.
 1399: This loses data and makes the origin invisible.
 1400: 
 1401: C<collision_prefix> changes this for secondary databases you designate.
 1402: When a secondary database at index N has a column that already exists in the
 1403: merged view, and C<collision_prefix-E<gt>{N}> is set, the colliding column is
 1404: published as C<"$prefix.$col"> instead of overwriting.  Both values are then
 1405: visible: the original column keeps its name (from the earlier database), and
 1406: the collision gets the prefixed name.
 1407: 
 1408: C<collision_prefix> is a hashref.  Each B<key> is the B<zero-based index> of
 1409: a secondary database in the C<databases> array (same numbering as C<join_map>).
 1410: Each B<value> is the prefix string to prepend.  An index-0 entry is
 1411: meaningless and silently ignored.  Omitting C<collision_prefix> entirely
 1412: preserves the previous last-wins behaviour and changes nothing.
 1413: 
 1414: Non-colliding columns from a secondary database are always added as-is with
 1415: no prefix, whether or not C<collision_prefix> is configured.
 1416: 
 1417: B<Example -- sales table and products table, both with a "product" column>
 1418: 
 1419:     # $sales    columns: id, product, amount, date
 1420:     # $products columns: sku, product, price, category
 1421:     # join on 'product' (left key) matched against 'sku' (right key via join_map)
 1422: 
 1423:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1424:         databases        => [$sales, $products],
 1425:         join_column      => 'product',
 1426:         join_map         => { 1 => 'sku' },
 1427:         collision_prefix => { 1 => 'products' },
 1428:     );
 1429: 
 1430:     $join->columns;
 1431:     # => ['amount', 'category', 'date', 'id', 'price', 'product', 'products.product']
 1432:     #                                                               ^-- prefixed collision
 1433: 
 1434:     my $row = $join->fetchrow_hashref(product => 'widget');
 1435:     # $row->{product}            -- value from $sales
 1436:     # $row->{'products.product'} -- value from $products (different row, same column name)
 1437:     # $row->{price}              -- from $products, no collision, kept as-is
 1438: 
 1439: B<Querying on a prefixed column>
 1440: 
 1441: Use the full published name as the criterion key:
 1442: 
 1443:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref('products.product' => 'widget');
 1444:     # Internally routes as: product => 'widget' to $products
 1445: 
 1446: B<Interaction with remove_column>
 1447: 
 1448: C<remove_column> operates on published names.  To suppress a prefixed
 1449: collision column entirely, pass the prefixed name:
 1450: 
 1451:     $join->remove_column('products.product');
 1452: 
 1453: =head2 backend - SQLite join backend for large datasets
 1454: 
 1455: C<Database::Join> can merge component databases in two different ways,
 1456: controlled by the C<backend> constructor parameter.
 1457: 
 1458: =over 4
 1459: 
 1460: =item C<backend =E<gt> 'array'> -- in-memory merge (original behaviour)
 1461: 
 1462: All matching rows are fetched from every component database into Perl hashes
 1463: and merged there.  Simple and fast for small and medium datasets.  Peak RAM
 1464: is roughly three times the combined source data size (one copy per database
 1465: plus one merged copy).
 1466: 
 1467: =item C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'> -- SQL JOIN via a cached temporary file
 1468: 
 1469: C<Database::Join> creates a temporary SQLite database file, spills source
 1470: rows into it (one table per component database), then executes a single SQL
 1471: C<JOIN> statement per query call.  Peak RAM drops to roughly one times the
 1472: source data size.
 1473: 
 1474: The temporary file is created once and reused across multiple query calls on
 1475: the same object (the cache).  Only query-time criteria vary per call; they
 1476: are applied as SQL C<WHERE> clauses against the cached data.  The cache is
 1477: automatically rebuilt when any source database's C<updated()> timestamp
 1478: changes.  The file is deleted when the object is destroyed (goes out of
 1479: scope).  See I<Temporary file: name, location, and lifetime> below for
 1480: details.
 1481: 
 1482: Requires C<DBD::SQLite E<gt>= 1.70> (C<FULL OUTER JOIN> support was added in
 1483: SQLite 3.39.0; DBD::SQLite 1.70 ships SQLite 3.39.2).
 1484: 
 1485: =item C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'> (default)
 1486: 
 1487: C<Database::Join> counts the total rows from all component databases cheaply
 1488: -- without fetching them -- and then decides:
 1489: 
 1490: =over 4
 1491: 
 1492: =item *
 1493: 
 1494: If the combined count is less than or equal to C<max_array_rows> (default
 1495: 10,000), use the array path.
 1496: 
 1497: =item *
 1498: 
 1499: If the combined count exceeds C<max_array_rows>, use the SQLite path.
 1500: 
 1501: =back
 1502: 
 1503: For counting to work without fetching, each component database must either
 1504: implement the C<dbi_source()> interface (for SQLite-backed sources, where a
 1505: C<COUNT(*)> SQL query is issued directly), or directly define a C<count()>
 1506: method in its own package -- not just inherit one from a parent class.  If
 1507: neither is available for a particular database, C<Database::Join> plays it
 1508: safe and uses the array path for the whole query without fetching any rows.
 1509: 
 1510: =back
 1511: 
 1512: B<Choosing max_array_rows>
 1513: 
 1514: The default of 10,000 is a reasonable starting point.  Adjust it to match
 1515: your hardware and typical row width.  For wide rows (many columns or long
 1516: strings) you may want a lower threshold; for narrow rows you can raise it.
 1517: 
 1518: B<Temporary file: name, location, and lifetime>
 1519: 
 1520: When the SQLite path is active, a single temporary SQLite database file acts
 1521: as the join cache for the life of the C<Database::Join> object.
 1522: 
 1523: B<Name>: the filename is randomly generated by C<File::Temp>, for example:
 1524: 
 1525:     /tmp/Cj8xK7mP2Q.db
 1526: 
 1527: The random portion (ten characters) is chosen automatically to avoid
 1528: collisions.  Only the directory is under your control; you cannot specify
 1529: the filename itself.
 1530: 
 1531: B<Location>: controlled by the C<tmpdir> constructor parameter.
 1532: If C<tmpdir> is not specified, C<File::Spec-E<gt>tmpdir()> is used (usually
 1533: C</tmp> on Unix, or the value of the C<TEMP> or C<TMP> environment variable
 1534: on Windows).
 1535: 
 1536: B<Lifetime: one file per object, deleted when the object is destroyed>: the
 1537: file is created on the first query call that uses the SQLite path and kept
 1538: alive until the C<Database::Join> object is destroyed (i.e. when it goes out
 1539: of scope or is explicitly C<undef>-d).  At most one file exists per object at
 1540: any given moment.  Calling C<selectall_arrayref()> ten times on the same
 1541: object creates and uses I<one> file, not ten.
 1542: 
 1543: B<Cache invalidation>: the cache is automatically rebuilt (the old file is
 1544: replaced with a new one) when any source database's C<updated()> return value
 1545: changes, or when C<add_database()> is called.  Base-filter criteria
 1546: (C<filters> constructor parameter) are applied once at build time for spilled
 1547: sources; query-time criteria are applied per-call as SQL C<WHERE> clauses.
 1548: 
 1549: To use a different directory -- for example a RAM-backed filesystem or a
 1550: faster local disk:
 1551: 
 1552:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1553:         databases      => [ $db1, $db2 ],
 1554:         join_column    => 'entry',
 1555:         backend        => 'sqlite',
 1556:         tmpdir         => '/dev/shm',     # Linux RAM disk
 1557:     );
 1558: 
 1559: B<Parallel secondary fetches (C<parallel> constructor parameter)>
 1560: 
 1561: By default, component databases are queried sequentially - the primary first,
 1562: then each secondary in order.  When the component databases are network- or
 1563: disk-backed and have non-trivial per-query latency, the sequential fetch means
 1564: total latency is the I<sum> of all per-DA latencies.
 1565: 
 1566: Setting C<< parallel => 1 >> in the constructor enables concurrent fetching
 1567: of secondary databases using Perl C<threads>.  With C<parallel => 1> and
 1568: two or more secondary databases (C<n E<gt> 2> total), secondary fetches run in
 1569: parallel after the primary fetch completes; total latency drops to
 1570: I<max(secondary latencies)> instead of I<sum(secondary latencies)>.
 1571: 
 1572:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1573:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty, $scores ],   # 3 DAs - 2 secondaries
 1574:         join_column => 'entry',
 1575:         parallel    => 1,    # loyalty and scores fetched concurrently
 1576:     );
 1577: 
 1578: Requirements and caveats:
 1579: 
 1580: =over 4
 1581: 
 1582: =item *
 1583: 
 1584: The C<threads> module must be available.  Most distributions ship it, but it
 1585: requires a Perl binary compiled with C<-Dusethreads>.  When threads are
 1586: unavailable, a C<carp> warning is emitted and fetching falls back to
 1587: sequential; the result is identical, only slower.
 1588: 
 1589: =item *
 1590: 
 1591: Parallel fetching is only active when the join has three or more total
 1592: databases (C<n E<gt> 2>).  With two databases (one secondary), the thread
 1593: creation overhead exceeds the benefit of concurrency; sequential is used
 1594: regardless of C<parallel>.
 1595: 
 1596: =item *
 1597: 
 1598: Component databases must be safe to call from Perl threads.  In-memory
 1599: databases (CSV, JSON, TSV after slurp) are safe.  DBI-backed databases
 1600: whose handles were created in the same thread may not be safe - consult your
 1601: DBD driver's thread documentation.  The array backend is recommended for
 1602: DBI-backed sources; the SQLite backend performs its join in a single SQL
 1603: statement and does not use parallel fetching.
 1604: 
 1605: =item *
 1606: 
 1607: C<parallel => 1> applies to the array backend only.  The SQLite backend
 1608: performs a single SQL JOIN after spilling source data, so per-DA parallelism
 1609: is irrelevant.
 1610: 
 1611: =back
 1612: 
 1613: B<Zero-copy ATTACH (C<dbi_source()> interface)>
 1614: 
 1615: Normally, when the SQLite path is active, rows from each component database
 1616: are fetched one by one and inserted into the temporary SQLite file.  This is
 1617: efficient but does involve INSERT overhead.
 1618: 
 1619: If a component database is itself SQLite-backed and implements a
 1620: C<dbi_source()> method, C<Database::Join> can skip the row-by-row copy
 1621: entirely and instead use C<ATTACH DATABASE> to link the source file directly
 1622: to the temporary join connection.  This is the zero-copy path and is
 1623: significantly faster for large SQLite sources.
 1624: 
 1625: The C<dbi_source()> method must return a hashref with two keys:
 1626: 
 1627: =over 4
 1628: 
 1629: =item C<dbh>
 1630: 
 1631: A connected C<DBD::SQLite> database handle (C<DBI> connection object).
 1632: 
 1633: =item C<table>
 1634: 
 1635: The name of the table in that database that holds the source rows.
 1636: 
 1637: =back
 1638: 
 1639: Example implementation:
 1640: 
 1641:     package My::SQLiteDatabase;
 1642:     use parent 'Database::Abstraction';
 1643: 
 1644:     sub dbi_source {
 1645:         my ($self) = @_;
 1646:         return {
 1647:             dbh   => $self->{_dbh},      # connected DBD::SQLite handle
 1648:             table => $self->{_table},    # table name in that database
 1649:         };
 1650:     }
 1651: 
 1652:     1;
 1653: 
 1654: The zero-copy ATTACH path is always used when a component database implements
 1655: C<dbi_source()>.  Query-time criteria are applied as parameterised SQL
 1656: C<WHERE> clauses against the ATTACHed source table, so no row-level copy is
 1657: needed even when the current call includes column filters.  (Prior to 0.005.0
 1658: any query-time criteria forced a spill; that restriction was removed in
 1659: 0.005.0.)
 1660: 
 1661: B<Result identity>
 1662: 
 1663: Both the array path and the SQLite path produce identical results for any
 1664: given query.  You can switch between them freely without changing callers.
 1665: The C<collision_prefix> column renaming, C<join_map> key translation, and all
 1666: three join types (left, inner, outer) work identically on both paths.
 1667: 
 1668: =head2 selectall_arrayref
 1669: 
 1670: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 1671: 
 1672:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1673:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier  => 'gold');
 1674:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(score => { '>' => 80 });
 1675:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref('C001');  # positional: entry => 'C001'
 1676:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(sort_by => 'name');
 1677:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold', sort_by => ['score', 'DESC']);
 1678:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(limit => 10);
 1679:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(limit => 10, offset => 20);
 1680:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold', sort_by => 'name', limit => 5);
 1681:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1, columns => ['tier']);
 1682:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1);  # distinct on all columns
 1683: 
 1684: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 1685: 
 1686: Returns an arrayref of hashrefs representing the merged view of all component
 1687: databases, optionally filtered by the given criteria.
 1688: 
 1689: Criteria for columns that live in different databases are routed
 1690: automatically: each database is queried with only the criteria that apply to
 1691: its own columns.  The results are combined in memory using C<join_column>.
 1692: 
 1693: Accepts the same criteria syntax as C<Database::Abstraction::selectall_arrayref>.
 1694: A single plain scalar argument is interpreted as the C<join_column> value
 1695: (equivalent to C<< entry => 'C001' >> when C<join_column> is C<'entry'>).
 1696: 
 1697: Pass C<< distinct => 1 >> (with optional C<< columns => \@cols >>) to
 1698: deduplicate results; see the C<distinct> feature section for details.
 1699: 
 1700: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 1701: 
 1702: =head4 Input
 1703: 
 1704:     Calling conventions (in order of precedence):
 1705:       1. No arguments             -- returns all rows
 1706:       2. One plain scalar         -- shorthand for join_column => $scalar
 1707:       3. Key-value pairs or
 1708:          a criteria hashref       -- routed per-database
 1709: 
 1710:     Values may be:
 1711:       Plain scalar                -- exact match
 1712:       Hashref of operators        -- e.g. { '>' => 80 }
 1713: 
 1714:     Optional parameters (mixed in with any of the above):
 1715:       sort_by => 'colname'            -- sort ascending by that column
 1716:       sort_by => ['colname', 'DESC']  -- sort descending
 1717:       sort_by => ['colname', 'ASC']   -- sort ascending (explicit)
 1718:       limit    => N                    -- return at most N rows (positive integer)
 1719:       offset   => M                   -- skip the first M rows (non-negative integer)
 1720: 
 1721:     The column named in sort_by must be present in the merged view (i.e. it
 1722:     must appear in columns()).  An unknown column or an invalid direction emits
 1723:     a carp warning and falls back to the default join_column ascending sort.
 1724: 
 1725:     limit and offset are applied after ordering.  offset without limit skips
 1726:     rows but returns all remaining rows.  limit without offset starts from
 1727:     the first qualifying row.  An invalid limit or offset emits a carp warning
 1728:     and the parameter is ignored (treated as absent).
 1729: 
 1730:     DOMAIN -- sort_by:
 1731:       EP absent:          result sorted by join_column ASC (default).
 1732:       EP string:          any column name in columns(); sorts ASC by that column.
 1733:       EP ['col','ASC']:   explicit ascending; equivalent to the string form.
 1734:       EP ['col','DESC']:  descending sort by the named column.
 1735:       EP ['col']:         single-element arrayref; direction defaults to ASC.
 1736:       EP []:              empty arrayref; column is undef -> carp + join_col ASC fallback.
 1737:       EP invalid column:  column not in columns() -> carp + join_col ASC fallback.
 1738:       EP invalid dir:     direction not 'ASC' or 'DESC' -> carp + ASC used.
 1739:       Sort is lexicographic (cmp); use backend=>'sqlite' for numeric ORDER BY.
 1740: 
 1741:     DOMAIN -- limit:
 1742:       EP absent:          no truncation; all qualifying rows are returned.
 1743:       EP 0:               not a positive integer -> carp + ignored (all rows returned).
 1744:       BVA min valid = 1:  exactly 1 row returned.
 1745:       BVA at count:       limit == total rows -> all rows returned (no truncation).
 1746:       BVA above count:    limit > total rows -> all rows returned.
 1747:       EP invalid:         negative integer, float string, or non-numeric string
 1748:                           => carp + ignored (all rows returned).
 1749:       Valid domain:       integers in [1, INF); matched by /^\d+\z/a with value >= 1.
 1750: 
 1751:     DOMAIN -- offset:
 1752:       EP absent:          no rows skipped; result starts from row 0.
 1753:       BVA min valid = 0:  no rows skipped (zero is a valid non-negative integer).
 1754:       BVA offset=1:       first row skipped; result starts from row 1.
 1755:       BVA offset=N-1:     N-1 rows skipped; only the last row returned.
 1756:       BVA offset=N:       all N rows skipped; empty result returned.
 1757:       BVA offset>N:       all rows skipped; empty result returned.
 1758:       EP invalid:         negative integer, float string, or non-numeric string
 1759:                           => carp + ignored (no rows skipped).
 1760:       Valid domain:       integers in [0, INF); matched by /^\d+\z/a.
 1761: 
 1762:     distinct => 1          -- deduplicate rows after the merge (see columns below)
 1763:     columns  => \@cols     -- arrayref of column names to project and deduplicate on;
 1764:                               used together with distinct => 1
 1765: 
 1766:     DOMAIN -- distinct:
 1767:       EP absent or 0:     no deduplication; all merged rows are returned.
 1768:       EP 1:               duplicates are removed (see below); first occurrence wins.
 1769:       EP truthy (not 1):  treated as truthy; deduplication is applied.
 1770:       Effect:             on the array backend, a Perl seen-hash keyed on the
 1771:                           selected column values is used; first occurrence wins.
 1772:                           On the SQLite backend, SELECT DISTINCT is used.
 1773: 
 1774:     DOMAIN -- columns:
 1775:       EP absent/undef:    when distinct is set, deduplication is on all columns
 1776:                           and full rows are returned.
 1777:       EP []:              empty arrayref treated the same as absent.
 1778:       EP valid:           arrayref of column names present in the merged view;
 1779:                           result rows are projected to those columns only, and
 1780:                           deduplication is on the projected values.
 1781:       EP with distinct=0: columns is ignored when distinct is absent or false.
 1782:       Note:               unknown column names in the list are silently ignored
 1783:                           at projection time; the row may lack those keys.
 1784: 
 1785: =head4 Output
 1786: 
 1787:     Arrayref of hashrefs; one hashref per qualifying merged row.
 1788:     Sorted ascending by join_column by default; caller-controlled via sort_by.
 1789:     At most C<limit> rows when limit is given; the first C<offset> rows are
 1790:     skipped when offset is given.
 1791:     Returns a reference to an empty array when no rows match.
 1792: 
 1793: =head3 EXAMPLE
 1794: 
 1795:     # All rows from both databases
 1796:     my $all = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1797: 
 1798:     # Only rows where the 'tier' column (from the loyalty database)
 1799:     # equals 'gold' -- the criterion is routed to the right database
 1800:     my $vip = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');
 1801: 
 1802:     # Operator hashref: score > 80
 1803:     my $high = $join->selectall_arrayref(score => { '>' => 80 });
 1804: 
 1805:     # Access each merged row
 1806:     for my $row (@{$vip}) {
 1807:         printf "%-10s tier=%-8s score=%d\n",
 1808:             $row->{entry}, $row->{tier}, $row->{score} // 0;
 1809:     }
 1810: 
 1811:     # Distinct tier values across all rows (project to one column, deduplicate)
 1812:     my $tiers = $join->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1, columns => ['tier']);
 1813:     # Returns e.g. [{ tier => 'gold' }, { tier => 'silver' }, { tier => 'bronze' }]
 1814:     # regardless of how many rows exist for each tier
 1815: 
 1816:     # Distinct region+tier combinations
 1817:     my $combos = $join->selectall_arrayref(
 1818:         distinct => 1,
 1819:         columns  => ['region', 'tier'],
 1820:     );
 1821: 
 1822:     # Distinct on all columns (remove fully-duplicate merged rows)
 1823:     my $deduped = $join->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1);
 1824: 
 1825: =head3 MESSAGES
 1826: 
 1827:     warn_unknown_column (carp)
 1828:         -- A criterion key names a column not present in any component database;
 1829:            the criterion is silently dropped and all rows are returned.
 1830:     error_join_criterion (croak)
 1831:         -- A criterion hashref contains a join => key (DA SQL-JOIN syntax).
 1832:            Use a separate DA object for the joined table instead.
 1833:     sort_by column unknown (carp)
 1834:         -- The column given in sort_by is not in the merged view; the result
 1835:            is returned in the default join_column ascending order instead.
 1836:     sort_by direction invalid (carp)
 1837:         -- The direction given in sort_by is not 'ASC' or 'DESC'; ASC is used.
 1838:     limit invalid (carp)
 1839:         -- The value given for limit is not a positive integer; it is ignored.
 1840:     offset invalid (carp)
 1841:         -- The value given for offset is not a non-negative integer; it is ignored.
 1842:     operator-unsupported (carp, SQLite/auto path only)
 1843:         -- An operator hashref key is not in the supported set (>, <, >=, <=,
 1844:            !=, =, LIKE, NOT LIKE, IS NULL, IS NOT NULL, IN, NOT IN); the
 1845:            individual operator term is dropped from the WHERE clause (other
 1846:            operators in the same hashref still apply).
 1847:     IN/NOT IN wrong value type (carp, SQLite/auto path only)
 1848:         -- An IN or NOT IN criterion was given a non-arrayref value; the operator
 1849:            term is skipped.
 1850:     error_sqlite_connect (croak, SQLite/auto path only)
 1851:         -- The temporary SQLite join file could not be created; check tmpdir
 1852:            permissions and available disk space.
 1853: 
 1854: =cut
 1855: 
 1856: sub selectall_arrayref {
 1857: 	my ($self, @args) = @_;
 1858: 	my $params   = $self->_parse_query_args(undef, @args);
 1859: 	my $sort_by  = delete $params->{sort_by};
 1860: 	my $limit    = delete $params->{limit};
 1861: 	my $offset   = delete $params->{offset};
 1862: 	my $distinct = delete $params->{distinct};
 1863: 	my $columns  = delete $params->{columns};
 1864: 	return $self->_joined_query($params,

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 1865: 		sort_by  => $sort_by, limit => $limit, offset => $offset,
 1866: 		distinct => $distinct, columns => $columns);
 1867: }
 1868: 
 1869: =head2 selectall_array
 1870: 
 1871: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 1872: 
 1873:     my @rows = $join->selectall_array(tier => 'gold');
 1874: 
 1875:     # Scalar context: only the first matching row
 1876:     my $first = $join->selectall_array(entry => 'C001');
 1877: 
 1878:     # Distinct column projection (list context)
 1879:     my @tiers = $join->selectall_array(distinct => 1, columns => ['tier']);
 1880: 
 1881:     # Distinct on all columns
 1882:     my @deduped = $join->selectall_array(distinct => 1);
 1883: 
 1884: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 1885: 
 1886: In list context returns a list of merged hashrefs -- the same rows that
 1887: C<selectall_arrayref> would return, just as a flat list rather than an
 1888: arrayref.
 1889: 
 1890: In scalar context returns only the first matching hashref (or C<undef> if
 1891: nothing matches).
 1892: 
 1893: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 1894: 
 1895: =head4 Input
 1896: 
 1897:     Same as selectall_arrayref.
 1898: 
 1899: =head4 Output
 1900: 
 1901:     List context:   list of hashrefs (may be empty).
 1902:     Scalar context: single hashref or undef.
 1903: 
 1904: =head3 EXAMPLE
 1905: 
 1906:     my @all = $join->selectall_array();
 1907:     print scalar @all, " rows\n";
 1908: 
 1909:     # First gold-tier customer only
 1910:     my $first_vip = $join->selectall_array(tier => 'gold');
 1911:     print $first_vip->{name}, "\n" if defined $first_vip;
 1912: 
 1913: =head3 MESSAGES
 1914: 
 1915: Same messages as C<selectall_arrayref>.
 1916: 
 1917: =cut
 1918: 
 1919: sub selectall_array {
 1920: 	my ($self, @args) = @_;
 1921: 	my $params   = $self->_parse_query_args(undef, @args);
 1922: 	my $sort_by  = delete $params->{sort_by};
 1923: 	my $limit    = delete $params->{limit};
 1924: 	my $offset   = delete $params->{offset};
 1925: 	my $distinct = delete $params->{distinct};
 1926: 	my $columns  = delete $params->{columns};
 1927: 	my $rows     = $self->_joined_query($params,
 1928: 		sort_by  => $sort_by, limit => $limit, offset => $offset,
 1929: 		distinct => $distinct, columns => $columns);
 1930: 	return wantarray ? @{$rows} : $rows->[0];

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 1931: }
 1932:

 1936: callers written against C<Database::Abstraction>'s deprecated API.  Use
 1937: C<selectall_arrayref> in new code.
 1938: 
 1939: =cut

 1952: 
 1953: =cut

 1957: 	carp 'Database::Join::selectall_hash is deprecated; use selectall_array';

 1960: 
 1961: =head2 fetchrow_hashref
 1962: 
 1963: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 1964: 
 1965:     my $row = $join->fetchrow_hashref(entry => 'C001');
 1966:     my $row = $join->fetchrow_hashref('C001');   # positional shorthand
 1967: 
 1968: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 1969: 
 1970: Returns a single merged hashref for the first row matching the given
 1971: criteria, or C<undef> when nothing matches.
 1972: 
 1973: Equivalent to calling C<selectall_arrayref> and taking only the first element.
 1974: All the same criteria conventions apply.
 1975: 
 1976: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 1977: 
 1978: =head4 Input
 1979: 
 1980:     Same as selectall_arrayref.
 1981: 
 1982: =head4 Output
 1983: 
 1984:     Hashref, or undef when no row matches.
 1985: 
 1986: =head3 EXAMPLE
 1987: 
 1988:     my $row = $join->fetchrow_hashref(entry => 'C001');

 1991:     } else {
 1992:         print "No record for C001\n";
 1993:     }

 1995:     # Positional: works when join_column is 'entry'
 1996:     my $row2 = $join->fetchrow_hashref('C001');

 1998: =head3 MESSAGES
 1999:

 2014: =head2 count

 2017: 
 2018:     my $total  = $join->count();

 2020: 
 2021: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2022: 
 2023: Returns the number of merged rows that satisfy the given criteria.
 2024: 
 2025: On the array backend, the full in-memory join is performed and the resulting
 2026: rows are counted in Perl.  On the SQLite backend, a C<SELECT COUNT(*)> SQL
 2027: query is executed against the cached join tables, avoiding a full row fetch.
 2028: 
 2029: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2030:

 2032:

 2034:

 2038:

 2040:

 2044: 
 2045:     printf "%d total, %d gold-tier, %d high-scorers\n",
 2046:         $total, $gold, $high;
 2047: 
 2048: =head3 MESSAGES
 2049: 
 2050: Same messages as C<selectall_arrayref>.
 2051: 
 2052: =cut

 2066: =head2 aggregate
 2067: 
 2068: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2069: 
 2070:     my $rows = $join->aggregate(
 2071:         by       => ['tier'],
 2072:         measures => [
 2073:             { col => 'score', fn => 'sum', as => 'total' },
 2074:             {                 fn => 'count', as => 'n'   },
 2075:         ],
 2076:         having   => { total => { '>' => 100 } },
 2077:         sort_by  => ['total', 'DESC'],
 2078:         limit    => 10,
 2079:     );
 2080:     # => [ { tier => 'gold', total => 183, n => 2 }, ... ]
 2081: 
 2082: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2083: 
 2084: Groups the merged view by one or more published column names and computes
 2085: aggregate measures over each group.
 2086: 
 2087: On the SQLite backend (C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'>) and the C<auto> backend,
 2088: the aggregation is pushed into the existing SQLite cache as a C<GROUP BY>
 2089: query - rows never pass through Perl.  The C<auto> backend always takes this
 2090: path for aggregation, skipping the row-count threshold probe (aggregation
 2091: makes the row count irrelevant).
 2092: 
 2093: On the array backend (C<backend =E<gt> 'array'>), merged rows are fetched in
 2094: full and folded in Perl.  This materialises the complete matching result set.
 2095: 
 2096: The two paths return the same rows for the same input, except for two
 2097: documented differences: C<min>/C<max> on a column declared C<TEXT> but
 2098: storing numbers (SQL compares lexically; Perl detects numerics), and
 2099: C<sum>/C<avg> with non-numeric values (SQL coerces to 0.0; Perl skips and
 2100: warns via C<warn_aggregate_non_numeric>).
 2101: 
 2102: C<Database::Join> has no C<no_fixate> and no CHI cache (unlike
 2103: C<Database::Abstraction>); aggregation results are not cached between calls.
 2104: 
 2105: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2106: 
 2107: =head4 Input
 2108: 
 2109: Reserved keys extracted before the rest is treated as WHERE criteria:
 2110: 
 2111:     by       => arrayref of published column names (default: [] = grand total)
 2112:     measures => arrayref of measure hashrefs (required, non-empty)
 2113:     having   => hashref of post-aggregation criteria
 2114:     sort_by  => 'col' or ['col', 'DESC']
 2115:     limit    => positive integer
 2116:     offset   => non-negative integer
 2117: 
 2118: Every other key is pre-aggregation criteria routed exactly as
 2119: C<selectall_arrayref> routes criteria.
 2120: 
 2121: Measure hashref keys:
 2122: 
 2123:     fn  => function name (sum|avg|min|max|count|count_distinct), case-insensitive
 2124:     col => published column name (required for all functions except bare count)
 2125:     as  => result key (required; must match C</^\w+\z/>; unique; not a by name)
 2126: 
 2127: C<having> uses the same operator vocabulary as C<selectall_arrayref>:
 2128: C<%SAFE_SQL_OPS> (E<gt>, E<lt>, E<gt>=, E<lt>=, !=, =, LIKE, NOT LIKE),
 2129: C<%SAFE_LIST_OPS> (IN, NOT IN), C<%SAFE_NOARG_OPS> (IS NULL, IS NOT NULL),
 2130: a bare scalar (= ?), and C<-or>/C<-and> groupings.  Write C<-between> as
 2131: two operators and C<LIKE> explicitly.
 2132: 
 2133: =head4 Output
 2134: 
 2135: Always an arrayref of hashrefs (never C<undef>).  C<[]> when no group
 2136: qualifies and C<by> is non-empty.  When C<by> is omitted or C<[]> and no
 2137: rows match, returns one all-normalised row (grand total of zero).
 2138: 
 2139: Key order per row: C<by> columns in declaration order, then measure aliases
 2140: in declaration order.
 2141: 
 2142: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2143: 
 2144:     # Total score and row count per tier, highest total first
 2145:     my $rows = $join->aggregate(
 2146:         by       => ['tier'],
 2147:         measures => [
 2148:             { col => 'score', fn => 'sum',   as => 'total' },
 2149:             {                 fn => 'count', as => 'n'     },
 2150:         ],
 2151:         sort_by  => ['total', 'DESC'],
 2152:     );
 2153:     for my $r (@{$rows}) {
 2154:         printf "%s: total=%s n=%s\n", $r->{tier}, $r->{total}, $r->{n};
 2155:     }
 2156: 
 2157: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
 2158: 
 2159:     1. get_params(undef, @args) -- hashref or kv-pairs only (no positional key)
 2160:     2. croak error_join_criterion if 'join =>' present
 2161:     3. _validate_aggregate_spec extracts by/measures/having/sort_by/limit/offset
 2162:     4. array backend:
 2163:          rows   = _joined_query_array(remaining criteria, no sort/limit/offset)
 2164:          groups = _fold_aggregate(rows, spec)
 2165:          groups = [grep { _match_criteria($_, having) } @groups] if having
 2166:          _normalise_aggregate(groups, spec.measures)
 2167:          _sort_and_page(groups, spec)
 2168:        sqlite/auto backend:
 2169:          _sqlite_aggregate(remaining criteria, spec)
 2170:     5. return groups
 2171: 
 2172: =head3 MESSAGES
 2173: 
 2174: C<error_aggregate_measures_required>, C<error_aggregate_measure_ref>,
 2175: C<error_aggregate_fn_unknown>, C<error_aggregate_col_required>,
 2176: C<error_aggregate_col_unknown>, C<error_aggregate_as_required>,
 2177: C<error_aggregate_as_invalid>, C<error_aggregate_as_duplicate>,
 2178: C<error_aggregate_as_collision>, C<error_aggregate_by_invalid>,
 2179: C<error_aggregate_having_key>, C<error_aggregate_having_operator>,
 2180: C<warn_aggregate_sort_key>, C<warn_aggregate_non_numeric>.
 2181: 
 2182: =cut
 2183:

 2215: 
 2216: =head2 each_row
 2217: 
 2218: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2219: 
 2220:     my $count = $join->each_row(sub { my ($row) = @_; ... });
 2221:     my $count = $join->each_row(sub { ... }, tier => 'gold');
 2222:     my $count = $join->each_row(sub { ... }, sort_by => 'name', limit => 100);
 2223: 
 2224: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2225: 
 2226: Calls C<\&callback> once for every row in the merged view that matches the
 2227: given criteria, then returns the total count of rows visited.
 2228: 
 2229: Accepts the same criteria, C<sort_by>, C<limit>, and C<offset> parameters as
 2230: C<selectall_arrayref>.
 2231: 
 2232: B<Note on memory usage>: C<Database::Join> always materialises the complete
 2233: merged result set before invoking the callback, because merging rows from
 2234: multiple independent sources requires that every source be queried and
 2235: cross-referenced first.  True constant-memory streaming (as C<Database::Abstraction>
 2236: provides on single-source SQL queries) is not possible at the join layer.
 2237: For genuinely large result sets use the SQLite backend (C<backend =E<gt>
 2238: 'sqlite'>), which keeps peak RAM to approximately one times the source data
 2239: size rather than three.
 2240: 
 2241: Any exception thrown inside the callback propagates to the caller after the
 2242: current row; subsequent rows are not visited.
 2243: 
 2244: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2245: 
 2246: =head4 Input
 2247: 
 2248:     \&callback   Positional coderef (required).
 2249:                  Called as $callback->($row_hashref) for each merged row.
 2250: 
 2251:     Additional arguments follow the same calling conventions as
 2252:     selectall_arrayref: no args, a single scalar join-column value,
 2253:     or key-value criteria pairs (including sort_by, limit, offset).
 2254: 
 2255: =head4 Output
 2256: 
 2257:     Non-negative integer: the count of rows for which the callback was invoked.
 2258: 
 2259: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2260: 
 2261:     # Print every gold-tier customer's name, sorted by name
 2262:     my $count = $join->each_row(
 2263:         sub { my ($row) = @_; print "$row->{name}\n" },
 2264:         tier    => 'gold',
 2265:         sort_by => 'name',
 2266:     );
 2267:     print "$count gold-tier customers\n";
 2268: 
 2269:     # Accumulate without holding the full result
 2270:     my $total_score = 0;
 2271:     $join->each_row(sub { $total_score += $_[0]->{score} // 0 });
 2272: 
 2273: =head3 MESSAGES
 2274: 
 2275:     error_invalid_callback (croak)
 2276:         -- First argument is not a code reference.
 2277: 
 2278: =cut
 2279:

 2296: =head2 dbi_source
 2297: 
 2298: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2299: 
 2300:     # Use a Database::Join object as a zero-copy SQLite source inside a
 2301:     # parent Database::Join, giving the parent ATTACHed-speed access to
 2302:     # the child's joined data without iterating through Perl.
 2303:     my $child  = Database::Join->new(databases => [$da, $db], join_column => 'id', backend => 'sqlite');
 2304:     my $parent = Database::Join->new(databases => [$child, $dc], join_column => 'id', backend => 'sqlite');
 2305: 
 2306: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2307: 
 2308: Returns a hashref C<< { dbh => $sqlite_dbh, table => '_dj_result' } >> that
 2309: allows a parent C<Database::Join> (or any other caller that understands the
 2310: C<dbi_source()> interface) to ATTACH the child's temporary SQLite database
 2311: file and query the materialised join result directly via SQL, without routing
 2312: rows through Perl.
 2313: 
 2314: The first call builds the SQLite cache (if not already current) and
 2315: materialises the full join result - with C<filters> applied but no query-time
 2316: criteria - into a real table named C<_dj_result> inside the cache file.
 2317: Subsequent calls within the same cache cycle reuse the existing table.
 2318: 
 2319: Returns C<undef> when the backend is C<'array'> (no SQLite file exists).
 2320: 
 2321: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2322: 
 2323: =head4 Input
 2324: 
 2325:     None.
 2326: 
 2327: =head4 Output
 2328: 
 2329:     On the SQLite/auto backend:
 2330:       Hashref with keys:
 2331:         dbh   => DBI handle to the child's temporary SQLite database file.
 2332:         table => '_dj_result'  (the materialized join table inside that file).
 2333:     On the array backend:
 2334:       undef
 2335: 
 2336: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2337: 
 2338:     # The parent automatically ATTACHes the child's SQLite file and queries
 2339:     # _dj_result for zero-copy composable nested joins.
 2340:     my $inner = Database::Join->new(
 2341:         databases   => [$customers, $loyalty],
 2342:         join_column => 'entry',
 2343:         backend     => 'sqlite',
 2344:     );
 2345:     my $outer = Database::Join->new(
 2346:         databases   => [$inner, $scores],
 2347:         join_column => 'entry',
 2348:         backend     => 'sqlite',
 2349:     );
 2350:     my $rows = $outer->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');
 2351: 
 2352: =head3 MESSAGES
 2353: 
 2354:     error_sqlite_connect (croak)
 2355:         -- The temporary SQLite join file could not be created.
 2356: 
 2357: =cut
 2358:

 2531: =head2 updated
 2532: 
 2533: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2534: 
 2535:     my $ts = $join->updated();
 2536: 
 2537: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2538: 
 2539: Returns the Unix timestamp of the most recent modification across all
 2540: component databases.  This is the maximum of all individual C<updated()>
 2541: return values.
 2542: 
 2543: Use this to implement simple cache-invalidation logic: if C<updated()>
 2544: has advanced since your last snapshot, re-query.
 2545: 
 2546: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2547: 
 2548: =head4 Input
 2549: 
 2550:     None.
 2551: 
 2552: =head4 Output
 2553: 
 2554:     Unix timestamp (positive integer).
 2555: 
 2556: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2557: 
 2558:     my $last_modified = $join->updated();
 2559:     if ($last_modified > $my_cache_timestamp) {
 2560:         $my_cache = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 2561:         $my_cache_timestamp = $last_modified;
 2562:     }
 2563: 
 2564: =head3 MESSAGES
 2565: 
 2566: C<updated()> does not emit any warnings or errors.  Component databases that do
 2567: not implement C<updated()>, or whose C<updated()> throws, are silently skipped;
 2568: only defined return values contribute to the maximum.  If no component database
 2569: implements C<updated()>, C<undef> is returned (same as C<List::Util::max> on an
 2570: empty list).
 2571: 
 2572: =cut
 2573: 
 2574: sub updated {
●2575 → 2577 → 2582 2575: 	my ($self) = @_;

 2578: 		my $ts;
 2579: 		do { local $@; $ts = eval { $db->updated() } };
 2580: 		push @timestamps, $ts if defined $ts;
 2581: 	}
 2582: 	return max(@timestamps);

 2585: =head2 set_logger
 2586: 
 2587: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2588: 
 2589:     $join->set_logger($log);
 2590:     $join->set_logger(logger => $log);   # named-pair form also accepted
 2591: 
 2592: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2593: 
 2594: Attaches a new logger object to the join and propagates it to every component
 2595: database.  The logger is used for diagnostic output by all component databases.
 2596: 
 2597: Non-blessed values (a log-level string such as C<"debug">, a filename, or a
 2598: code reference) are wrapped in C<Log::Abstraction->new(...)> automatically,
 2599: matching the behaviour of C<Database::Abstraction::set_logger>.
 2600: 
 2601: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2602: 
 2603: =head4 Input
 2604: 
 2605:     logger  Positional or named: a logger object, log-level string, filename,
 2606:             or code reference (required).  Non-blessed values are wrapped in
 2607:             Log::Abstraction automatically.
 2608: 
 2609: =head4 Output
 2610: 
 2611:     Returns C<$self> for method chaining.
 2612: 
 2613: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2614: 
 2615:     # Log::Any is used here as an example; any object that implements
 2616:     # debug() and info() (or whichever methods your component databases
 2617:     # call internally) works equally well.
 2618:     use Log::Any qw($log);
 2619: 
 2620:     my $join = Database::Join->new(databases => [$db1, $db2], join_column => 'entry');
 2621:     $join->set_logger($log);
 2622:     # $log is now used by $join and by $db1 and $db2
 2623: 
 2624:     # Named-pair form (mirrors Database::Abstraction API):
 2625:     $join->set_logger(logger => $log);
 2626: 
 2627: =head3 MESSAGES
 2628: 
 2629:     (croak) Usage: set_logger(logger => $logger)
 2630:         -- Called with an undefined argument.  Pass a valid logger object.
 2631: 
 2632: =cut

 2652: =head2 add_database
 2653: 
 2654: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2655: 
 2656:     # Positional: database object as first argument
 2657:     $join->add_database($db);
 2658: 
 2659:     # Named: equivalent to the above
 2660:     $join->add_database(database => $db);
 2661: 
 2662:     # With options (mixed positional + named)
 2663:     $join->add_database($db, remove_columns => ['internal_id']);
 2664:     $join->add_database($db, join_column    => 'local_key_name');
 2665:     $join->add_database($db, filter         => { score => { '>' => 60 } });
 2666: 
 2667:     # Chainable
 2668:     $join->add_database($db1)->add_database($db2, remove_columns => ['notes']);
 2669: 
 2670: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2671: 
 2672: Adds one more C<Database::Abstraction> subclass object to the logical view
 2673: and immediately updates the column-ownership index.
 2674: 
 2675: After the call, all query methods return rows that include columns from the
 2676: newly added database, and criteria on those new columns are routed to it
 2677: automatically.
 2678: 
 2679: When a column name in the new database already exists in an earlier database,
 2680: the new database becomes the authoritative source for that column
 2681: (last-database-wins, the same rule that applies at construction time).
 2682: 
 2683: The join-column must be present in the new database (or declared via
 2684: C<join_column>).  The logger is propagated to the new database if one is set.
 2685: 
 2686: C<add_database> is the runtime equivalent of listing the database in the
 2687: C<databases> array to C<new>.  The optional C<join_column> parameter is
 2688: equivalent to a C<join_map> entry; the optional C<filter> parameter is
 2689: equivalent to a C<filters> entry.
 2690: 
 2691: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2692: 
 2693: =head4 Input
 2694: 
 2695:     database       => { type => 'object',   required => 1 }
 2696:                       # A Database::Abstraction subclass instance.
 2697:                       #
 2698:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   blessed object that passes
 2699:                       #                       isa('Database::Abstraction').
 2700:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: non-reference, unblessed ref, wrong class,
 2701:                       #                       or non-reference non-key scalar (the guard at
 2702:                       #                       the top of add_database rejects it with
 2703:                       #                       error_invalid_db before validate_strict runs).
 2704: 
 2705:     join_column    => { type => 'string',   optional => 1 }
 2706:                       # The name of the join key in THIS new database,
 2707:                       # when it differs from the canonical join_column.
 2708:                       #
 2709:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any string that exists as a column in the
 2710:                       #                       new database.
 2711:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: string absent from the new database's columns()
 2712:                       #                       => croak error_join_col_missing.
 2713: 
 2714:     filter         => { type => 'hashref',  optional => 1 }
 2715:                       # Permanent criteria for this database only.
 2716:                       # Same format as selectall_arrayref.
 2717:                       #
 2718:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   hashref of criteria (may be {} for no-op).
 2719:                       # DOMAIN -- EP absent:  no permanent filter applied; all rows visible.
 2720:                       # DOMAIN -- Key-set:    a non-empty filter makes this DB an inner-join
 2721:                       #                       partner regardless of the outer join_type.
 2722: 
 2723:     remove_columns => { type => 'arrayref', optional => 1 }
 2724:                       # Column names from this database to hide.
 2725:                       #
 2726:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   arrayref of strings; non-existent columns silently
 2727:                       #                       ignored; empty [] is a safe no-op.
 2728:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: join_column itself => croak error_remove_join_col.
 2729: 
 2730: =head4 Output
 2731: 
 2732:     Returns C<$self> to support method chaining.
 2733: 
 2734: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2735: 
 2736:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 2737:         databases   => [ $customers ],
 2738:         join_column => 'entry',
 2739:     );
 2740: 
 2741:     # Add loyalty data; hide internal columns from it
 2742:     $join->add_database($loyalty, remove_columns => ['audit_ts']);
 2743: 
 2744:     # Add score data; only include rows with score > 60
 2745:     $join->add_database($scores, filter => { score => { '>' => 60 } });
 2746: 
 2747:     # Add a database whose join key has a different local name
 2748:     $join->add_database($stnames, join_column => 'state_code');
 2749: 
 2750:     # All three options combined, and chained
 2751:     $join->add_database($db4,
 2752:         join_column    => 'ref_id',
 2753:         filter         => { active => 1 },
 2754:         remove_columns => ['legacy_col'],
 2755:     );
 2756: 
 2757: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
 2758: 
 2759:     determine the new database's index (length of current _dbs array)
 2760:     extract the database object from positional or named argument
 2761:     croak if it is not a Database::Abstraction subclass
 2762:     register join_column alias in _join_map if different from canonical
 2763:     register filter in _filters if provided
 2764:     fetch column list from the new database
 2765:     croak if the join key is missing from the new database
 2766:     append the new database to _dbs and _db_cols
 2767:     update _col_db: for each new column, point it at the new index
 2768:         (last-database-wins; skip removed columns and the local join alias)
 2769:     invalidate _col_cache and _schema_cache
 2770:     propagate logger if set
 2771:     apply remove_columns if provided
 2772:     return $self
 2773: 
 2774: =head3 MESSAGES
 2775: 
 2776:     error_invalid_db          -- argument is not a Database::Abstraction subclass
 2777:     error_join_col_missing    -- join_column not found in the new database
 2778:     warn_schema_type_mismatch -- (carp) the new database has a shared column whose type
 2779:                                  differs from the type already in the view; use
 2780:                                  collision_prefix to preserve both values
 2781: 
 2782: =cut

 2890: =head2 remove_column
 2891: 
 2892: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2893: 
 2894:     $join->remove_column('email');
 2895: 
 2896:     # Chainable
 2897:     $join->remove_column('internal_id')->remove_column('audit_ts');
 2898: 
 2899: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2900: 
 2901: Permanently hides a column from the merged view.  After this call:
 2902: 
 2903: =over 4
 2904: 
 2905: =item *
 2906: 
 2907: The column does not appear in C<columns()> or C<schema()>.
 2908: 
 2909: =item *
 2910: 
 2911: Returned row hashrefs do not contain the column key.
 2912: 
 2913: =item *
 2914: 
 2915: Any query criterion that references the removed column is silently dropped
 2916: (with a C<carp> warning).
 2917: 
 2918: =back
 2919: 
 2920: The C<join_column> cannot be removed; attempting to do so will C<croak>.
 2921: Removing a column that does not exist in any database is silently ignored
 2922: (the call is idempotent and safe).  The C<columns()> and C<schema()>
 2923: memoisation caches are cleared automatically.
 2924: 
 2925: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2926: 
 2927: =head4 Input
 2928: 
 2929:     $col    Positional string: the column name to remove.
 2930: 
 2931:             DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any string; non-existent columns are silently
 2932:                                   ignored (idempotent call, returns $self).
 2933:             DOMAIN -- EP invalid: join_column value => croak error_remove_join_col.
 2934:             DOMAIN -- BVA:        undef and '' are explicit no-ops (returns $self).
 2935:                                   These are below the minimum meaningful string
 2936:                                   length and are handled without any warning.
 2937: 
 2938: =head4 Output
 2939: 
 2940:     Returns C<$self> to support method chaining.
 2941: 
 2942: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2943: 
 2944:     # Hide private fields immediately after construction
 2945:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 2946:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
 2947:         join_column => 'entry',
 2948:     )->remove_column('email')
 2949:      ->remove_column('internal_notes');

 2951:     # Verify they are gone
 2952:     my $cols = $join->columns();

 2954: 
 2955: =head3 MESSAGES
 2956: 
 2957:     error_remove_join_col -- attempt to remove the join_column itself
 2958: 
 2959: =cut

 3028: 
 3029: =head2 AUTOLOAD - column shortcut
 3030: 
 3031: Calling an unknown method whose name matches a visible column name performs
 3032: a column lookup across the merged view.
 3033: 
 3034: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 3035: 
 3036:     # Scalar context: value from the first matching row
 3037:     my $name  = $join->name(entry => 'C001');
 3038: 
 3039:     # List context: values from every matching row
 3040:     my @tiers = $join->tier();
 3041: 
 3042:     # With a positional join-key argument (when join_column is 'entry')
 3043:     my $score = $join->score('C001');
 3044: 
 3045: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 3046: 
 3047: AUTOLOAD routes the call to the appropriate component database by looking up
 3048: the column name in the internal column-ownership index.
 3049: 
 3050: When either C<join_map> or C<filters> is active, AUTOLOAD performs a full
 3051: join query instead of delegating directly to the owning database.  This is
 3052: necessary because:
 3053: 
 3054: =over 4
 3055: 
 3056: =item *
 3057: 
 3058: With C<join_map>, the owning database's primary key may differ from the
 3059: canonical join key used in the call arguments.
 3060: 
 3061: =item *
 3062: 
 3063: With C<filters>, bypassing the join would return rows that the filter is
 3064: meant to exclude.
 3065: 
 3066: =back
 3067: 
 3068: In list context, every matching merged row contributes one value to the
 3069: returned list.  In scalar context, only the first row's value is returned.
 3070: 
 3071: Calling a method whose name begins with C<_> (a private method) via AUTOLOAD
 3072: will C<croak> with a clear error message rather than being silently ignored.
 3073: 
 3074: =head3 EXAMPLE
 3075: 
 3076:     # Lookup a single customer's name (scalar context)
 3077:     my $name = $join->name('C001');   # 'C001' maps to entry => 'C001'
 3078:     print "Name: $name\n";
 3079: 
 3080:     # Get every tier value in the view (list context)
 3081:     my @all_tiers = $join->tier();

 3413: # _joined_query_array( \%params ) -> \@merged_rows
 3414: #
 3415: # Purpose: Core in-memory join algorithm.  Partitions criteria, fetches per-database
 3416: #          results, resolves the key set, and merges rows.
 3417: #
 3418: # Key-set resolution (applied for each secondary database after the primary):
 3419: #
 3420: #   If the database had criteria in this query call (after base filter overlay),
 3421: #   it acts as an INNER-JOIN partner: only keys present in its filtered result
 3422: #   survive.  This gives WHERE-clause semantics even under a LEFT join.
 3423: #
 3424: #   If the database had NO effective criteria:
 3425: #     inner  -> intersect  (standard inner join)
 3426: #     left   -> no change  (primary defines the key set)
 3427: #     outer  -> union      (all keys from any database)
 3428: #
 3429: # Row merge: for each qualifying primary row, secondary rows are overlaid in
 3430: # index order.  For duplicate columns, later databases win.  Local join-key
 3431: # aliases are renamed to the canonical join_column before merging.
 3432: # Removed columns are deleted from every merged row.

 5189: # _copy_criteria( \%criteria ) -> \%copy
 5190: # Purpose: Return a two-level deep copy of a single criteria hashref so that
 5191: #          post-construction mutation of the caller's hash cannot change the
 5192: #          stored filter.  Operator sub-hashrefs (e.g. { '>' => 80 }) are
 5193: #          shallow-copied one additional level, matching the broadcast-copy
 5194: #          idiom used in _partition_criteria for join-column criteria.
 5195: # Entry:   $criteria is a hashref (may be undef).
 5196: # Exit:    Returns a new hashref; never returns the input reference itself.
 5197: sub _copy_criteria {
 5198: 	my ($criteria) = @_;
 5199: 	return {} unless $criteria && %{$criteria};
 5200: 	return {
 5201: 		map {
 5202: 			$_ => ref($criteria->{$_}) eq 'HASH'
 5203: 				? { %{ $criteria->{$_} } }  # shallow-copy operator sub-hashref
 5204: 				: $criteria->{$_}
 5205: 		} keys %{$criteria}
 5206: 	};
 5207: }

 5209: # _copy_filters( \%filters ) -> \%copy

 5219: }

 5225: # Exit:    Returns the formatted string.
