 5242: 
 5243: =head1 ENCODING
 5244: 
 5245: C<Database::Join> is a routing and merge layer written in pure Perl.  It never
 5246: inspects, re-encodes, or transforms the I<content> of strings -- only their
 5247: identity (via C<eq> / hash-key equality) and, on the array backend, their
 5248: numeric interpretation (via C<looks_like_number>).
 5249: 
 5250: =over 4
 5251: 
 5252: =item Column names
 5253: 
 5254: Column names are exactly what each component C<Database::Abstraction> object
 5255: returns from its own C<columns()> method.  C<Database::Join> compares them with
 5256: C<eq> (byte-level equality) and builds its internal routing index from them.
 5257: ASCII column names (letters, digits, underscore) are safe everywhere.
 5258: Non-ASCII column names work as long as every component database returns them
 5259: in the same byte encoding and your Perl source file is loaded in the same
 5260: encoding.  Mixed encodings between component databases are not supported.
 5261: 
 5262: =item Join-key values (the join_column)
 5263: 
 5264: The join-column value is used as a hash key internally (C<$hash{$key}>).
 5265: Perl uses byte-level equality for hash keys, so any value that round-trips
 5266: correctly through your component databases works as a join key, including
 5267: non-ASCII strings, emoji, and other Unicode text, as long as all component
 5268: databases return the same bytes for the same conceptual key.
 5269: 
 5270:     # A Japanese city name as a join key works fine
 5271:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(entry => "\x{6771}\x{4EAC}");
 5272: 
 5273: =item Criteria values and row data
 5274: 
 5275: Criterion values and merged row values are passed verbatim between the caller
 5276: and the component databases.  C<Database::Join> does not examine them.  Full
 5277: UTF-8 text (including multi-byte characters, accented letters, CJK ideographs,
 5278: and emoji) round-trips safely provided the underlying component databases handle
 5279: UTF-8 correctly.
 5280: 
 5281:     # Emoji in a criterion value -- works if the DA supports it
 5282:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(mood => "\x{1F600}");
 5283: 
 5284: =item SQLite backend: UTF-8 round-trip guarantee
 5285: 
 5286: When the SQLite path is active (C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'> or C<'auto'>),
 5287: values are inserted into the temporary C<DBD::SQLite> database exclusively
 5288: through DBI prepared-statement placeholders -- never by string interpolation.
 5289: C<DBD::SQLite> operates in UTF-8 mode by default (C<sqlite_unicode =E<gt> 1>
 5290: in newer releases), so multi-byte UTF-8 text round-trips without corruption.
 5291: Binary blobs containing null bytes may not round-trip correctly; use the array
 5292: backend if you need binary-safe joins.
 5293: 
 5294: =item Sorting on the array backend uses Perl string comparison
 5295: 
 5296: The array backend sorts with Perl's C<cmp> (byte-level lexicographic order).
 5297: This is correct for pure-ASCII data and for consistently-encoded UTF-8 data
 5298: where byte order matches Unicode code-point order (e.g. sorting within a
 5299: single script).  For locale-aware sorting (e.g. sorting German umlauts in
 5300: German alphabetical order) switch to the SQLite backend, which delegates to
 5301: SQLite's C<ORDER BY> (locale support depends on how SQLite was compiled).
 5302: 
 5303: =item Measure aliases in aggregate()
 5304: 
 5305: The C<as> alias for each measure must match C</^\w+\z/a> -- ASCII word
 5306: characters only (letters, digits, underscore).  Full Unicode identifiers
 5307: are not supported as aliases.
 5308: 
 5309: =item i18n messages
 5310: 
 5311: All internal error and warning messages route through the optional C<i18n>
 5312: object via a C<translate($key, @args)> call.  The translation dictionary
 5313: controls the final encoding and character set of those user-visible strings.
 5314: 
 5315: =back
 5316: 
 5317: =head1 MESSAGES
 5318: 
 5319: The following messages can be produced by C<Database::Join>.  All messages
 5320: can be localised by supplying an C<i18n> object to C<new>.
 5321: 
 5322: =over 4
 5323: 
 5324: =item C<error_no_databases>
 5325: 
 5326: B<When:> The C<databases> arrayref passed to C<new> is empty.
 5327: 
 5328: B<Fix:> Pass at least one C<Database::Abstraction> subclass object.
 5329: 
 5330: =item C<error_invalid_db>
 5331: 
 5332: B<When:> An element of the C<databases> array (or the argument to
 5333: C<add_database>) is not an object, or is not a C<Database::Abstraction>
 5334: subclass.
 5335: 
 5336: B<Fix:> Instantiate the component database with its own C<new> method before
 5337: passing it to C<Database::Join>.
 5338: 
 5339: =item C<error_join_col_missing>
 5340: 
 5341: B<When:> The join key column (or its C<join_map> alias) does not exist in one
 5342: of the component databases.
 5343: 
 5344: B<Fix:> Either add the column to the database, change C<join_column> to a
 5345: column that is present everywhere, or use C<join_map> to declare the local
 5346: alias for databases that call it something different.
 5347: 
 5348: =item C<error_remove_join_col>
 5349: 
 5350: B<When:> C<remove_column> is called with the name of the join key column.
 5351: 
 5352: B<Fix:> The join key is required for the merge to work and cannot be hidden.
 5353: Remove a different column.
 5354: 
 5355: =item C<error_invalid_prefix>
 5356: 
 5357: B<When:> A value in the C<collision_prefix> hashref is a reference (e.g. a
 5358: hashref or arrayref) rather than a plain string.
 5359: 
 5360: B<Fix:> All C<collision_prefix> values must be plain strings.  A reference
 5361: would be stringified to C<HASH(0x...)> or C<ARRAY(0x...)>, leaking a heap
 5362: address into every column name returned by C<columns()>, C<schema()>, and all
 5363: query results.  Pass a plain string such as C<'db2'> or C<'secondary'>.
 5364: 
 5365: =item C<warn_unknown_column> (carp)
 5366: 
 5367: B<When:> A criterion is passed for a column that does not exist in any
 5368: component database (or has been removed with C<remove_column>).
 5369: 
 5370: B<Fix:> Check the column name spelling.  The criterion is ignored.
 5371: 
 5372: =item C<error_join_criterion>
 5373: 
 5374: B<When:> A criterion hashref contains a C<join =E<gt>> key (the
 5375: C<Database::Abstraction> SQL-JOIN syntax).
 5376: 
 5377: B<Fix:> C<join =E<gt>> targets a single table inside one DA; it cannot be
 5378: routed through a merged view.  Express multi-table relationships by adding
 5379: the joined table as a separate C<Database::Abstraction> object in the
 5380: C<databases =E<gt> []> constructor list instead.
 5381: 
 5382: =item C<error_query_unsupported>
 5383: 
 5384: B<When:> C<query()> is called on a C<Database::Join> object.
 5385: 
 5386: B<Fix:> Use C<selectall_arrayref>, C<selectall_array>, C<fetchrow_hashref>,
 5387: or C<count> instead.
 5388: 
 5389: =item C<error_group_by_unsupported>
 5390: 
 5391: B<When:> C<group_by()> is called on a C<Database::Join> object.
 5392: 
 5393: B<Fix:> Use C<aggregate()> instead.  The fluent C<Database::Abstraction::Query>
 5394: chain targets a single component DA, not the merged view.
 5395: 
 5396: =item C<error_execute_unsupported>
 5397: 
 5398: B<When:> C<execute()> is called on a C<Database::Join> object.
 5399: 
 5400: B<Fix:> Use the Perl-level query methods instead.  Raw SQL cannot span
 5401: heterogeneous database backends.
 5402: 
 5403: =item C<error_invalid_backend>
 5404: 
 5405: B<When:> The C<backend> parameter passed to C<new> is not one of C<'array'>,
 5406: C<'sqlite'>, or C<'auto'>.
 5407: 
 5408: B<Fix:> Use exactly one of those three strings.  The check is case-sensitive;
 5409: C<'SQLite'> or C<'Auto'> will not be accepted.
 5410: 
 5411: =item C<error_sqlite_connect>
 5412: 
 5413: B<When:> The SQLite join backend fails to open the temporary SQLite database
 5414: file.  Common causes: the C<tmpdir> directory is not writable, the filesystem
 5415: has no free space, or C<DBD::SQLite> is not installed.
 5416: 
 5417: B<Fix:> Check that the directory given by C<tmpdir> (or the system temp
 5418: directory if C<tmpdir> was not set) is writable and has sufficient free space.
 5419: Verify that C<DBD::SQLite> 1.70 or later is installed.
 5420: 
 5421: =item C<error_aggregate_measures_required>
 5422: 
 5423: B<When:> C<aggregate()> is called without a C<measures> key, or with
 5424: C<measures => []>.
 5425: 
 5426: B<Fix:> Pass a non-empty arrayref: C<< measures => [{ fn => 'count', as => 'n' }] >>.
 5427: 
 5428: =item C<error_aggregate_measure_ref>
 5429: 
 5430: B<When:> One of the elements in the C<measures> arrayref is not a hashref (it
 5431: is a scalar, arrayref, or other non-hash reference).
 5432: 
 5433: B<Fix:> Every element of C<measures> must be a plain hashref with at least
 5434: C<fn> and C<as> keys.
 5435: 
 5436: =item C<error_aggregate_fn_unknown>
 5437: 
 5438: B<When:> The C<fn> value in a measure hashref is not one of: C<sum>, C<avg>,
 5439: C<min>, C<max>, C<count>, C<count_distinct> (case-insensitive).
 5440: 
 5441: B<Fix:> Use a supported function name.  C<median>, C<stddev> and window
 5442: functions are not implemented.
 5443: 
 5444: =item C<error_aggregate_col_required>
 5445: 
 5446: B<When:> The C<col> field is missing from a measure whose C<fn> requires a
 5447: column (any function except plain C<count>).
 5448: 
 5449: B<Fix:> Add C<< col => 'column_name' >> to the measure hashref.  C<count>
 5450: without C<col> emits C<COUNT(*)>; all other functions require a column.
 5451: 
 5452: =item C<error_aggregate_col_unknown>
 5453: 
 5454: B<When:> The C<col> in a measure, or a name in C<by>, refers to a column that
 5455: is not in the merged view (either never existed or was hidden with
 5456: C<remove_column>).
 5457: 
 5458: B<Fix:> Check C<< $join->columns() >> for the correct published name.
 5459: C<collision_prefix>-renamed columns are accessed as C<"prefix.col">.
 5460: 
 5461: =item C<error_aggregate_as_required>
 5462: 
 5463: B<When:> A measure hashref has no C<as> key, or its value is C<undef> or the
 5464: empty string.
 5465: 
 5466: B<Fix:> Every measure must have a non-empty C<as> alias: C<< as => 'total' >>.
 5467: 
 5468: =item C<error_aggregate_as_invalid>
 5469: 
 5470: B<When:> A measure alias (C<as>) contains characters other than word
 5471: characters (C<\w>: letters, digits, underscore).
 5472: 
 5473: B<Fix:> Use a plain word for the alias: C<< as => 'total' >>, not
 5474: C<< as => 'total score' >> or C<< as => 'total-score' >>.
 5475: 
 5476: =item C<error_aggregate_as_duplicate>
 5477: 
 5478: B<When:> Two or more measures share the same C<as> alias.
 5479: 
 5480: B<Fix:> Choose a unique alias for each measure.
 5481: 
 5482: =item C<error_aggregate_as_collision>
 5483: 
 5484: B<When:> A measure's C<as> alias is the same as one of the C<by> column names.
 5485: 
 5486: B<Fix:> Choose an alias that does not match any grouping dimension.
 5487: 
 5488: =item C<error_aggregate_by_invalid>
 5489: 
 5490: B<When:> The C<by> parameter is present but is not an arrayref.
 5491: 
 5492: B<Fix:> Pass an arrayref (possibly empty for a grand total):
 5493: C<< by => ['region'] >> or C<< by => [] >>.
 5494: 
 5495: =item C<error_aggregate_having_key>
 5496: 
 5497: B<When:> A key in the C<having> hashref is not one of the C<by> column names,
 5498: a measure alias, C<-or>, or C<-and>.
 5499: 
 5500: B<Fix:> Only filter on names that appear in the aggregate output.
 5501: 
 5502: =item C<error_aggregate_having_operator>
 5503: 
 5504: B<When:> An operator hashref inside C<having> uses an operator that is not in
 5505: C<%SAFE_SQL_OPS>, C<%SAFE_LIST_OPS>, or C<%SAFE_NOARG_OPS>.
 5506: 
 5507: B<Fix:> Use a supported operator.  C<BETWEEN> is not supported; write it as
 5508: two constraints: C<< { '>=' => $lo, '<=' => $hi } >>.
 5509: 
 5510: =item C<warn_aggregate_sort_key> (carp)
 5511: 
 5512: B<When:> The column or alias named by C<sort_by> in C<aggregate()> is not one
 5513: of the C<by> columns or measure aliases.
 5514: 
 5515: B<Fix:> Use a column name from C<by> or a measure alias from C<measures>.
 5516: The sort falls back to the default group ordering (C<by> columns ascending).
 5517: 
 5518: =item C<warn_aggregate_non_numeric> (carp)
 5519: 
 5520: B<When:> The array backend encounters one or more non-numeric values in a
 5521: column that is the target of a C<sum> or C<avg> measure.  The SQLite backend
 5522: coerces non-numeric values to C<0.0> via C<CAST> and emits no warning.
 5523: 
 5524: B<Fix:> Clean the data before joining, or switch to the SQLite backend if
 5525: coercion to zero is acceptable.
 5526: 
 5527:     # Example: the warning names the column, the count, and the first bad value
 5528:     # "aggregate: column "score" has 2 non-numeric value(s), skipped, e.g. "n/a""
 5529: 
 5530: =back

 5551: 
 5552: =head1 SECURITY CONSIDERATIONS
 5553: 
 5554: C<Database::Join> is a pure in-memory routing and merge layer.  It never
 5555: generates SQL strings, never opens files, and never calls C<system()>,
 5556: C<exec()>, or C<eval()>.  The security properties described below are
 5557: architectural guarantees, not run-time checks.
 5558: 
 5559: =head2 What Database::Join guarantees
 5560: 
 5561: =over 4
 5562: 
 5563: =item Criteria partition isolation
 5564: 
 5565: Every criterion you pass to a query method is routed to I<exactly one>
 5566: component database (the one that owns that column), or to I<all> databases
 5567: when the criterion is on the join key column.  A hostile value in a criterion
 5568: for column C<name> (owned by database A) will never reach database B.
 5569: 
 5570: =item Unknown columns are rejected before reaching any database
 5571: 
 5572: If a criterion column name is not present in any component database (or has
 5573: been hidden with C<remove_column>), C<Database::Join> logs a C<carp> warning
 5574: and silently drops the criterion.  No database receives the hostile key.
 5575: 
 5576: =item AUTOLOAD only accepts word-character column names
 5577: 
 5578: Perl's method dispatch extracts the column name via C<\w+>, which matches only
 5579: C<[A-Za-z0-9_]>.  Hostile method names with shell metacharacters, quotes, or
 5580: spaces cannot reach the AUTOLOAD dispatch path.  Private names (starting with
 5581: C<_>) are additionally blocked with an explicit C<croak>.
 5582: 
 5583: =item No value sanitisation (by design)
 5584: 
 5585: C<Database::Join> does I<not> sanitise, HTML-encode, or validate the
 5586: I<values> in criteria hashrefs.  Preventing SQL injection is the
 5587: responsibility of the underlying C<Database::Abstraction> objects (which use
 5588: parameterised queries).  Preventing XSS or header injection is the
 5589: responsibility of the CGI or web layer that renders the output.
 5590: 
 5591: =item Taint-mode compatible (array path)
 5592: 
 5593: The array merge path contains no C<system()>, C<exec()>, backtick,
 5594: C<open(PIPE)>, or C<eval STRING> calls.  It neither opens files nor constructs
 5595: shell commands.  The AUTOLOAD regex C</ :: (\w++) \z /x> uses a possessive
 5596: quantifier (C<\w++>) and a strict end-of-string anchor (C<\z>) and produces
 5597: an I<untainted> capture, so the column name used for dispatch is clean under
 5598: C<-T>.  Criteria values are passed verbatim to component C<Database::Abstraction>
 5599: objects; those objects are responsible for handling tainted values at the SQL
 5600: parameterisation layer.
 5601: 
 5602: =item SQLite backend: column names are quoted, values are parametrised
 5603: 
 5604: When the SQLite path is active, C<Database::Join> generates SQL internally.
 5605: All column and table names are double-quoted (SQL identifier quoting) before
 5606: being embedded in statement strings.  All row values are passed to SQLite
 5607: exclusively through DBI prepared statement placeholders -- never by string
 5608: interpolation.  A hostile value in a source row therefore cannot inject SQL
 5609: into the temporary database.
 5610: 
 5611: The temporary SQLite file is created by C<File::Temp> using a securely random,
 5612: unpredictable filename.  No C<system()> or shell command is used to create or
 5613: remove it.  The connection is made with C<DBI-E<gt>connect(..., { RaiseError =E<gt> 1,
 5614: PrintError =E<gt> 0 })> and is closed before the method returns.  Column names
 5615: in the generated SQL come from C<columns()>, which is produced by
 5616: C<Database::Abstraction> at construction time -- they are not derived from
 5617: caller-supplied criteria values.
 5618: 
 5619: =item Operator hashref broadcast copy
 5620: 
 5621: When the same join-key criterion (an operator hashref such as
 5622: C<< { '>' => 'A' } >>) is broadcast to multiple component databases, each
 5623: database receives its own I<shallow copy> of the hashref.  A component database
 5624: that mutates the hashref's contents at the top level cannot affect what
 5625: subsequent databases receive.
 5626: 
 5627: =item collision_prefix value type guard
 5628: 
 5629: C<_build_col_index> rejects any C<collision_prefix> value that is a reference
 5630: (hashref, arrayref, coderef, etc.) with an immediate C<croak>.  A reference
 5631: value would stringify to C<"HASH(0x...)">, leaking a heap address into every
 5632: column name, C<columns()> listing, and merged row returned to the caller.  The
 5633: guard fires before any column name is constructed.
 5634: 
 5635: =item Filter deep-copy isolation
 5636: 
 5637: The C<filters> constructor parameter and the C<filter> option of
 5638: C<add_database()> are I<deep-copied> at the point of use.  The caller's
 5639: original hashrefs are never stored; post-construction mutation of those
 5640: hashrefs cannot widen or bypass the configured row-security constraints.
 5641: 
 5642: =item mandatory_filters cannot be overridden or bypassed at query time
 5643: 
 5644: Predicates stored in C<_mandatory_filters> are applied unconditionally to every
 5645: query result, after the in-memory merge (array path) or as parameterised SQL
 5646: C<WHERE> clauses (SQLite path).  There is no query-time parameter that suppresses
 5647: or modifies them.  Caller-supplied criteria are ANDed with the mandatory
 5648: predicates -- they can only further restrict the result set, never widen it.
 5649: 
 5650: Each predicate is a shallow copy of the caller-supplied hashref; post-construction
 5651: mutation of the original C<mandatory_filters> arrayref or its elements has no
 5652: effect on the stored predicates.
 5653: 
 5654: Values are always passed to the SQLite engine via DBI bind parameters, never by
 5655: string interpolation.  A hostile C<val> in a predicate cannot inject SQL.
 5656: 
 5657: =back
 5658: 
 5659: =head2 What the caller is responsible for
 5660: 
 5661: =over 4
 5662: 
 5663: =item Sanitise values before building criteria
 5664: 
 5665: DJ passes criterion values verbatim to component databases.  If your
 5666: application accepts user-supplied filter values (e.g. from a CGI query
 5667: string), those values I<must> be validated or sanitised by your application
 5668: before being passed to DJ.
 5669: 
 5670: =item Restrict which columns the caller can filter on
 5671: 
 5672: Any column in C<columns()> can be used as a filter criterion.  If a column
 5673: should not be filterable by end users (e.g. an internal status flag), hide it
 5674: with C<remove_column> so that queries on it are silently dropped.
 5675: 
 5676: =item Do not expose the joined view directly to user-supplied criteria
 5677: 
 5678: DJ is not a firewall.  It faithfully routes user input to component databases.
 5679: Wrap DJ calls in a thin service layer that whitelists the permitted criterion
 5680: columns and validates their values.
 5681: 
 5682: =back
 5683: 
 5684: =head3 API SPECIFICATION (security surface)
 5685: 
 5686:     Input accepted by all query methods and passed through DJ to component databases:
 5687: 
 5688:     Criterion values:
 5689:         type: scalar string | operator hashref { OP => scalar }
 5690:         validation: NONE (DJ trusts the caller; component DA is responsible)
 5691:         max size: unconstrained (OOM risk on very large values)
 5692: 
 5693:     Column name keys in criteria:
 5694:         type: string
 5695:         validation: must be present in _col_db (else carp + drop)
 5696:         character set: any Perl string (including control chars); DJ does
 5697:                        not impose a character-set restriction on criteria KEYS
 5698: 
 5699:     AUTOLOAD method-name-as-column:
 5700:         type: \w+ (enforced by Perl regex /::(\w+)$/)
 5701:         validation: must not start with '_'; must be in _col_db
 5702: 
 5703: =encoding UTF-8
 5704: 
 5705: =head1 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 5706: 
 5707: Z calculus schemas for the key invariants and operations.
 5708: Unicode is used throughout this section as required by Z notation.
 5709:

 5803:     collision_prefix : â„• â‡¸ STRING
 5804:     dbs              : seq DATABASE_ABSTRACTION
 5805:     col_db           : NAME â‡¸ â„•
 5806:     â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€

 5808:     dom collision_prefix âŠ† 1 â€¥ (#dbs - 1)
 5809: 
 5810:     -- Published name for column col from database i:
 5811:     published(i, col) ==
 5812:         if i âˆˆ dom collision_prefix âˆ§ col âˆˆ dom col_db âˆ§ col_db(col) < i
 5813:         then (collision_prefix i) ^ "." ^ col
 5814:         else col
 5815:

 5817:     col_db(published(i, col)) = i

 5819:     -- The original column name is never removed from an earlier database:
 5820:     âˆ€ i : 1 â€¥ #dbs-1; col : columns(dbs i) â€¢
 5821:         published(i, col) â‰  col  âŸ¹

 5866:     -- A filtered database i always contributes to key-set intersection.

 5876:             if col âˆˆ dom base âˆ© dom extra
 5877:                âˆ§ base(col) âˆˆ HASHREF âˆ§ extra(col) âˆˆ HASHREF
 5878:             then col â†¦ base(col) âˆª extra(col)   -- operator union
 5879:             else col â†¦ (if col âˆˆ dom extra then extra(col) else base(col)) }

 5881: =head2 base_criteria
 5882: 
 5883:     â”€â”€â”€ BaseCriteria â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5884:     base_criteria : CRITERIA              -- column-name keyed
 5885:     col_db        : NAME â‡¸ â„•
 5886:     filters       : â„• â‡¸ CRITERIA         -- after Init
 5887:     â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5888:     -- base_criteria is applied once at construction by partitioning
 5889:     -- its columns into per-db slices and merging into filters:
 5890:     âˆ€ i : 0 â€¥ #dbs-1 â€¢
 5891:         bc_slice(i) = partition(base_criteria, i)
 5892:         filters(i)  = merge_criteria(bc_slice(i), filters_explicit(i))
 5893: 
 5894:     -- where filters_explicit is the filters parameter as supplied.
 5895:     -- merge_criteria semantics: explicit filters win on plain-scalar
 5896:     -- conflicts; operator hashrefs are combined (AND).
 5897: 
 5898:     -- Unknown columns are dropped (warn_unknown_column carp);
 5899:     -- the join_column is broadcast to all databases.
 5900: 
 5901:     -- Key-set semantics: any database that receives a non-empty
 5902:     -- bc_slice acts as an inner-join partner (same as filters).

 5904: =head2 aggregate
 5905: 
 5906:     â”€â”€â”€ AggregateSpec â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5907:     MEASURE ::= { fn : AGGFN; col : NAME?; as : ALIAS }
 5908:     AGGFN   ::= sum | avg | min | max | count | count_distinct
 5909:     ALIAS   ::= \w+
 5910:     SPEC    ::= { by : seq NAME; measures : seq MEASURE;
 5911:                   having : CRITERIA?;
 5912:                   sort_by : NAME Ã— {ASC,DESC}?; limit : â„•?; offset : â„•? }
 5913:     â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5914:     -- Pre-conditions (checked by _validate_aggregate_spec):
 5915:     âˆ€ m : measures â€¢
 5916:         m.fn âˆˆ {sum, avg, min, max, count, count_distinct}
 5917:         âˆ§ (m.fn â‰  count âˆ¨ m.fn = count_distinct âŸ¹ m.col â‰  âˆ…)
 5918:         âˆ§ m.as matches /^\w+\z/
 5919:     âˆ€ name : by â€¢ name âˆˆ dom col_db
 5920:     âˆ€ m : measures â€¢ m.col â‰  âˆ… âŸ¹ m.col âˆˆ dom col_db
 5921:     # aliases are unique and do not collide with 'by' names:
 5922:     âˆ€ i,j : 0 â€¥ #measures-1 â€¢ i â‰  j âŸ¹ measures(i).as â‰  measures(j).as
 5923:     âˆ€ m : measures; b : by â€¢ m.as â‰  b
 5924: 
 5925:     â”€â”€â”€ Aggregate â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5926:     ÎžDatabase_Join               -- state unchanged (Xi-transition)
 5927:     criteria?  : CRITERIA        -- pre-aggregation WHERE
 5928:     spec?      : SPEC
 5929:     result!    : seq GROUP_ROW
 5930:     â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 5931:     -- GROUP_ROW has exactly the by-columns plus the measure aliases.
 5932:     -- Group key: tuple of by-column values from a merged row.
 5933:     group_key(row, by) == { b : by â€¢ b â†¦ row(b) }
 5934: 
 5935:     -- Fold function on the array backend:
 5936:     fold(rows, spec) ==
 5937:         let G = { k : IMAGE(group_key(r, by) | r : rows) â€¢ k â†¦ {} }
 5938:         for r : rows:
 5939:             G[group_key(r, by)] += r   -- accumulate per measure.fn
 5940:         for g : dom G:
 5941:             G[g] = { b : by â€¢ b â†¦ g(b) }
 5942:                  âˆª { m : measures â€¢ m.as â†¦ apply(m.fn, m.col, G[g].rows) }
 5943:         result_pre = ran G
 5944: 
 5945:     -- apply: maps aggregate function to its computed value.
 5946:     apply(count, âˆ…, rows)         == #rows
 5947:     apply(count_distinct, col, rows) == #{ r : rows â€¢ r(col) }
 5948:     apply(sum, col, rows)         == Î£ { r : rows | is_num(r(col)) â€¢ r(col) }
 5949:     apply(avg, col, rows)         == sum(col, rows) / #{ r | is_num(r(col)) }
 5950:     apply(min, col, rows)         == min { r : rows â€¢ r(col) }
 5951:     apply(max, col, rows)         == max { r : rows â€¢ r(col) }
 5952: 
 5953:     -- Post-aggregation HAVING filter:
 5954:     result_having = [ g : result_pre | matches_having(g, spec.having) ]
 5955: 
 5956:     -- Grand total (by = []):
 5957:     #by = 0 âŸ¹ #result! = 1
 5958:     #by > 0 âˆ§ #result_having = 0 âŸ¹ result! = []
 5959: 
 5960:     -- Final result:
 5961:     result! = sort_and_page(result_having, spec)
 5962: 
 5963:     -- Backend dispatch:
 5964:     backend âˆˆ {sqlite, auto} âŸ¹ aggregation pushed to SQLite GROUP BY
 5965:     backend = array           âŸ¹ fold() applied in Perl
 5966: 
 5967:     -- Correctness invariant (both paths return identical rows):
 5968:     âˆ€ criteria?, spec? â€¢
 5969:         _sqlite_aggregate(criteria?, spec?) = _fold_aggregate(rows(criteria?), spec?)
 5970: 
 5971: =head2 selectall_arrayref
 5972: 
 5973:     selectall_arrayref : CRITERIA â†’ seq MERGED_ROW
 5974:     pre:  âˆ€ col : dom criteria â€¢ col âˆˆ dom self._col_db âˆª {self._join_col}
 5975:     post: result = _joined_query(criteria)
 5976:           result is sorted ascending by join_col value
 5977: 
 5978: =head2 selectall_array
 5979: 
 5980:     selectall_array : CRITERIA â†’ seq MERGED_ROW | MERGED_ROW?
 5981:     pre:  same as selectall_arrayref
 5982:     post: wantarray  => result = @{ selectall_arrayref(criteria) }
 5983:           !wantarray => result = selectall_arrayref(criteria)[0]  (or undef)
 5984: 
 5985: =head2 fetchrow_hashref
 5986: 
 5987:     fetchrow_hashref : CRITERIA â†’ MERGED_ROW?
 5988:     post: result = selectall_arrayref(criteria)[0]  (or undef if empty)
 5989: 
 5990: =head2 count
 5991: 
 5992:     count : CRITERIA â†’ â„•
 5993:     post: result = #selectall_arrayref(criteria)
 5994: 
 5995: =head2 columns
 5996: 
 5997:     columns : â†’ seq NAME
 5998:     post: result = sort(
 5999:               (â‹ƒ { i : 0 â€¥ #dbs-1 â€¢ ran(dbs(i).columns) }
 6000:                \ dom removed_cols
 6001:                \ { local_jc(i) | i âˆˆ dom join_map âˆ§ local_jc(i) â‰  join_col })
 6002:           )
 6003: 
 6004: =head2 schema
 6005: 
 6006:     schema : â†’ NAME â‡¸ SCHEMA_INFO
 6007:     post: dom(result) = ran(columns())
 6008:           âˆ€ col : dom(result) â€¢
 6009:               result(col) = (last database containing col).schema()(col)
 6010: 
 6011: =head2 updated
 6012: 
 6013:     updated : â†’ â„•
 6014:     post: result = max { i : 0 â€¥ #dbs-1 â€¢ dbs(i).updated() }
 6015: 
 6016: =head2 remove_column
 6017: 
 6018:     remove_column : NAME â†’ Database_Join
 6019:     pre:  col â‰  self._join_col
 6020:     post: self'._removed_cols = self._removed_cols âˆª {col}
 6021:           self'._col_db       = self._col_db \ {col}
 6022:           self'._col_cache    = undef
 6023:           self'._schema_cache = undef
 6024: 
 6025: =head2 AUTOLOAD
 6026: 
 6027:     AUTOLOAD : NAME Ã— CRITERIA â†’ VALUE | seq VALUE
 6028:     pre:  col âˆˆ dom self._col_db
 6029:           col does not begin with '_'
 6030:     post: let rows = _joined_query(criteria)
 6031:           wantarray  => result = { r : rows â€¢ r(col) }
 6032:           !wantarray => result = rows(0)(col)  (or undef if rows is empty)
 6033: 
 6034: =head2 backend
 6035: 
 6036:     â”€â”€â”€ BackendDispatch â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 6037:     backend        : {array, sqlite, auto}
 6038:     max_array_rows : â„•
 6039:     â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
 6040: 
 6041:     -- row_count(db): cheaply count rows in a component database.
 6042:     -- Uses dbi_source() COUNT(*) SQL for SQLite-backed sources,
 6043:     -- or the DA's own count() method when defined in its own package.
 6044:     -- Returns âŠ¥ (bottom / unknown) when neither is available.
 6045:     row_count(db) ==
 6046:         if (db has dbi_source() returning a SQLite dbh)
 6047:         then SELECT COUNT(*) FROM source_table
 6048:         else if (defined &{ref(db) ^ "::count"})
 6049:         then db.count()
 6050:         else âŠ¥
 6051: 
 6052:     -- Combined row count across all component databases.
 6053:     -- If any database returns âŠ¥, total is âŠ¥ (cannot determine).
 6054:     total_count ==
 6055:         if âˆ€ i : 0 â€¥ #dbs-1 â€¢ row_count(dbs i) â‰  âŠ¥
 6056:         then Î£ { i : 0 â€¥ #dbs-1 â€¢ row_count(dbs i) }
 6057:         else âŠ¥
 6058: 
 6059:     -- Dispatch rule for _joined_query:
 6060:     use_sqlite(C) ==
 6061:         backend = 'sqlite'
 6062:         âˆ¨ (backend = 'auto' âˆ§ total_count â‰  âŠ¥ âˆ§ total_count > max_array_rows)
 6063: 
 6064:     _joined_query(C) ==
 6065:         if use_sqlite(C)
 6066:         then _sqlite_join(C)
 6067:         else _joined_query_array(C)
 6068: 
 6069:     -- Result identity invariant: both paths return identical rows.
 6070:     âˆ€ C : CRITERIA â€¢
 6071:         _sqlite_join(C) = _joined_query_array(C)
 6072: 
 6073: =head1 STATE DIAGRAM
 6074: 
 6075: C<Database::Join> objects follow three independent finite state machines (FSMs).
 6076: Each FSM is described with an ASCII diagram showing valid states (boxes), the
 6077: triggers that cause transitions (arrows), and important side-effects.
 6078: 
 6079: =head2 FSM 1: Object Lifecycle
 6080: 
 6081: Governs the structural state of a C<Database::Join> instance.
 6082: Query methods (C<selectall_arrayref>, C<selectall_array>, C<fetchrow_hashref>,
 6083: C<count>, C<aggregate>, C<each_row>, C<columns>, C<schema>, C<updated>) are
 6084: schema-preserving (Xi-transitions) and are not shown because they do not change
 6085: state.  C<aggregate()> additionally triggers the SQLite cache lifecycle (FSM 2)
 6086: when the backend is C<'sqlite'> or C<'auto'>, just like any other query method.
 6087: 
 6088:     [pre-creation]
 6089:          |
 6090:          | new( databases => [...], join_column => '...' )
 6091:          |   Side-effect: _col_db routing table built;
 6092:          |                _autoload_pk set from autoload_pk param (default: join_column)
 6093:          v
 6094:     [CONSTRUCTED] <-----------------------------------------+
 6095:          |    |                                              |
 6096:          |    +--------------------------------------------+ |
 6097:          |    (query methods: no structural change)         | |
 6098:          |                                                  | |
 6099:          |-- remove_column( col ) -------> [COL_REMOVED] <--+ |
 6100:          |                                      |    |        |
 6101:          |   Side-effect: col removed from       |    |        |
 6102:          |   _col_db; _col_cache and             +----+        |
 6103:          |   _schema_cache cleared.              (idempotent;  |
 6104:          |   Join column cannot be removed.)      chainable)   |
 6105:          |                                                     |
 6106:          +-- add_database( db ) ----------> [DB_ADDED] <------+
 6107:                                                 |    |
 6108:              Side-effect: new columns added;    |    | add_database( db )
 6109:              _col_db extended; SQLite cache     |    | (chainable; each
 6110:              invalidated (if any).              +----+  extends the view)
 6111: 
 6112:     Note: COL_REMOVED and DB_ADDED are not mutually exclusive.
 6113:     Both transitions are legal on any valid object, in any order.
 6114: 
 6115:     Illegal triggers (always croak; object state is not changed):
 6116: 
 6117:       Trigger                              Error
 6118:       -----------------------------------  ---------------------------------
 6119:       new( databases => [] )               error_no_databases
 6120:       remove_column( join_column )         error_remove_join_column
 6121:       add_database( non-reference )        error_invalid_database
 6122:       new() with join_col absent from DB   error_join_col_absent
 6123: 
 6124: =head2 FSM 2: SQLite Cache Lifecycle
 6125: 
 6126: Governs the temporary SQLite cache used by the C<backend='sqlite'> and
 6127: C<backend='auto'> join paths.  The cache does not exist until the first
 6128: query on the SQLite path.
 6129: 
 6130:     [ABSENT] <----- add_database( db )
 6131:        |                  |
 6132:        |  (no temp file)  | Side-effect: old DBI handle disconnected;
 6133:        |                  |   _sqlite_cache deleted.
 6134:        |                  |
 6135:        |                  +<-------------------------------------------+
 6136:        |                                                               |
 6137:        | first query on SQLite path                                    |
 6138:        | Side-effect: File::Temp db created in tmpdir;                 |
 6139:        |   DBI connected; sources ATTACHed or spilled;                 |
 6140:        |   _sqlite_cache = { dbh, tmpfile, n, updated, ... }           |
 6141:        v                                                               |
 6142:     [FRESH] <--+                                                       |
 6143:        |        |                                                      |
 6144:        |        | subsequent queries                                   |
 6145:        |        | (cache reused; refaddr of _sqlite_cache unchanged)   |
 6146:        +--------+                                                      |
 6147:        |                                                               |
 6148:        | updated() timestamp of any source DA changes                  |
 6149:        | -- OR -- source row count changes                             |
 6150:        | Side-effect: none yet (_cache_fresh returns false)            |
 6151:        v                                                               |
 6152:     [STALE]                                                            |
 6153:        |                                                               |
 6154:        | next query                                                    |
 6155:        | Side-effect: old DBI handle disconnected; old temp file       |
 6156:        |   unlinked; new temp file built from current source data.     |
 6157:        +---------------------------------------------------------------+
 6158:        (transitions to FRESH)
 6159: 
 6160:     On object DESTROY:
 6161:       FRESH/STALE:  DBI handle disconnected; File::Temp object released
 6162:                     (temp file unlinked by File::Temp DESTROY).
 6163:       ABSENT:       No temp file exists; no-op.
 6164: 
 6165: =head2 FSM 3: Column Visibility (per column)
 6166: 
 6167: Each column in the logical view independently follows a two-state machine.
 6168: Transition from VISIBLE to REMOVED is one-way: C<add_database> never
 6169: restores a column that is in C<_removed_cols>.
 6170: 
 6171:     [VISIBLE] <-- initial state for every column at construction
 6172:          |    |
 6173:          |    | query / columns() / schema()
 6174:          |    | (column present in results; no state change)
 6175:          +----+
 6176:          |
 6177:          | remove_column( col )
 6178:          | Side-effect: col deleted from _col_db;
 6179:          |   _col_cache and _schema_cache cleared.
 6180:          v
 6181:     [REMOVED] <---+
 6182:          |         |
 6183:          |         | remove_column( col ) again
 6184:          |         | (idempotent; no error; no second side-effect)
 6185:          +---------+
 6186: 
 6187:     One-way invariant:
 6188:       If col is in _removed_cols, then add_database( db_that_has_col )
 6189:       does NOT re-add it.  Formal: col_db' = col_db âŠ• { c | c in
 6190:       ran(db.columns) \ {local_jc} \ removed }.
 6191: 
 6192:     Illegal trigger:
 6193:       remove_column( join_column )  -- error_remove_join_column (croaks;
 6194:                                        state unchanged)
 6195:
